Stable vector bundles on a hyper-Kähler manifold with a rank 1 1 obstruction map are modular
Eyal Markman

Abstract
. Let X X be an irreducible 2 ​ n 2n -dimensional holomorphic symplectic manifold. A reflexive sheaf F F is very modular , if its Azumaya algebra ℰ ​ n ​ d ​ ( F ) {\mathcal{E}}nd(F) deforms with X X to every Kähler deformation of X X . We show that if F F is a slope-stable reflexive sheaf of positive rank and the obstruction map H ​ H 2 ​ ( X ) → Ext 2 ​ ( F , F ) HH^{2}(X)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{2}(F,F) has rank 1 1 , then F F is very modular. We associate to such a sheaf a vector in the Looijenga-Lunts-Verbitsky lattice of rank b 2 ​ ( X ) + 2 b_{2}(X)+2 . Three sources of examples of such modular sheaves emerge. The first source consists of slope-stable reflexive sheaves F F of positive rank which are isomorphic to the image Φ ⁡ ( 𝒪 X ) \Phi({\mathcal{O}}_{X}) of the structure sheaf via an equivalence Φ : D b ​ ( X ) → D b ​ ( Y ) \Phi:D^{b}(X)\rightarrow D^{b}(Y) of the derived categories of two irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifolds. The second source consists of such F F , which are isomorphic to the image of a sky-scraper sheaf via a derived equivalence. The third source consists of images Φ ⁡ ( L ) \Phi(L) of torsion sheaves L L supported as line bundles on holomorphic lagrangian submanifolds Z Z , such that Z Z deforms with X X in co-dimension one in moduli and L L is a rational power of the canonical line bundle of Z Z . An example of the first source is constructed using a stable and rigid vector bundle G G on a K ​ 3 K3 surface X X to get the very modular vector bundle F F on the Hilbert scheme X [ n ] X^{[n]} , associated to the equivariant vector bundle G ⊠ ⋯ ⊠ G G\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes G on X n X^{n} via the BKR-correspondence. This builds upon and partially generalizes results of K. O’Grady for n = 2 n=2 [ O2 ] . A construction of the second source associates to a set { G i } i = 1 n \{G_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n} of n n distinct stable vector bundles in the same two dimensional moduli space of vector bundles on a K ​ 3 K3 surface X X the very modular vector bundle F F on X [ n ] X^{[n]} corresponding to the equivariant bundle ⊕ σ ∈ 𝔖 n [ G σ ⁡ ( 1 ) ⊠ ⋯ ⊠ G σ ⁡ ( n ) ] \oplus_{\sigma\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{n}}[G_{\sigma(1)}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes G_{\sigma(n)}] on X n X^{n} .



2010 Mathematics Subject Classification
14C25,14D15,14D20

1.
Introduction

1.1.
The obstruction map

1.1.1.
The obstruction map of objects in the derived category

An
irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold
is a simply connected compact Kähler manifold
X
such
that
H^{0}(X,\Omega^{2}_{X})
is one-dimensional spanned by an everywhere non-degenerate holomorphic
2
-form.
If
X
is
2
-dimensional, then it is a
K3
surface. It is well known that if
E
is a slope-stable vector bundle
over a
K3
surface
X
, then the projective bundle
{\mathbb{P}}(E)
deforms with
X
to every complex deformation of
X

[
Ve4
]
. The analogous statement fails if
\dim(X)>2
even to first order, since
c_{2}({\mathcal{E}}nd(E))

need not remain of Hodge type
(2,2)
. The starting point of this paper is the observation that an analogous statement holds for torsion free reflexive sheaves satisfying the infinitesimal condition that the obstruction homomorphism

(1.1)

{\rm obs}_{E}:HH^{2}(X)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{2}(E,E)

has rank
1
(Theorem
1.2
). Above,
HH^{2}(X)
is the second Hochschild cohomology of
X
, considered as the space of natural transformations from the identity functor
id:D^{b}(X)\rightarrow D^{b}(X)
to its shift
id[2]
, and
{\rm obs}_{E}
sends
a natural transformation
\alpha
to its value
\alpha_{E}
on
E
.
The space
HH^{2}(X)
parametrizes first order deformations of the category
\mbox{Coh}(X)
of coherent sheaves on
X

and those yield also first order deformations of the derived category
D^{b}(X)

[
To
]
. The class

{\rm obs}_{E}(\xi)

is the obstruction to lifting
\xi\in HH^{2}(X)
to a first order deformation of the pair
(D^{b}(X),E)
(see
[
To
, Prop. 6.1]
).

Given an object
F\in D^{b}(X)
of non-zero rank
r
, set
\kappa(F):=ch(F)\exp\left(\frac{-c_{1}(F)}{r}\right)
.
If
F
is a locally free coherent sheaf, then
\kappa(F)
is a characteristic class of the projective bundle
{\mathbb{P}}(F)
.
We first observe that if
X
is a projective irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold and
F
is an object in
D^{b}(X)
with
{\rm obs}_{F}
of rank
1
, then
\kappa(F)
remains a Hodge class for all Kähler deformations of
X
(see Lemma
2.3
).

Given an equivalence of derived categories
\Phi:D^{b}(X)\rightarrow D^{b}(Y)
, we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{HH^{2}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi_{*}}
\scriptstyle{{\rm obs}_{F}}
\textstyle{HH^{2}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm obs}_{\Phi(F)}}
\textstyle{{\rm Hom}(F,F[2])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\textstyle{{\rm Hom}(\Phi(F),\Phi(F)[2]),}

where the horizontal arrows are isomorphisms, and so the rank of
{\rm obs}_{\Phi(F)}
and
{\rm obs}_{F}
are equal. If
{\rm rank}(F)\neq 0
,
then the restriction of
{\rm obs}_{F}
to the subspace
H^{0}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X})
of
HH^{2}(X)
is injective, by
[
HL
, Lemma 10.1.3]
. Thus,
{\rm rank}({\rm obs}_{F})\geq 1
for every object
F
such that
{\rm rank}(\Phi(F))\neq 0
, for some equivalence of derived categories
\Phi:D^{b}(X)\rightarrow D^{b}(Y)
.

1.1.2.
Objects with a rank
1
obstruction map

Three sources
1
1

1

The rank of
{\rm obs}_{F}
is
1
for every
{\mathbb{P}}^{n}
-object
F
, since then
\dim{\rm Hom}(F,F[2])=\dim H^{2}({\mathbb{P}}^{n})=1
. However, all examples of
{\mathbb{P}}^{n}
-objects we are aware of arise via one of sources (1) or (3) listed via equivalences of derived categories and deformations.
of objects
F
with
{\rm obs}_{F}
of rank
1
emerge:

(1)

F=\Phi({\mathcal{O}}_{X})
, where
\Phi:D^{b}(X)\rightarrow D^{b}(Y)
is an equivalence of derived categories of two projective irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifolds. Note that
{\rm Ext}^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{X},{\mathcal{O}}_{X})
is one dimensional and so
{\rm obs}_{F}
has rank
1
.

(2)

F
is the image of a sky-scraper sheaf of a point via an equivalence
\Phi
as above (See Section
3.1
).

(3)

F
is the image
\Phi(\iota_{*}L)
via an equivalence
\Phi
as above, where
\iota:Z\hookrightarrow X

is the embedding of
Z
as a holomorphic lagrangian submanifold and
L
is a
line bundle on
Z
,
such that

(a)

\iota^{*}:H^{1,1}(X)\rightarrow H^{1,1}(Z)
has rank
1
, and

(b)

L
is a rational power of the canonical line bundle of
Z
.

Sources (
1
) and (
2
) above seem to be special cases of deformations of source (
3
). If, for exampe,
X
is the Hilbert scheme
S^{[n]}
of
n
points on a
K3
surface
S
and
S
contains a smooth rational curve
C
,
then
{\mathcal{O}}_{S^{[n]}}
is the image via an auto-equivalence of
D^{b}(S^{[n]})
of the structure sheaf
{\mathcal{O}}_{C^{[n]}}
of the lagrangian projective space
C^{[n]}
(see Example
3.12
). Similarly, assume that
S
admits an elliptic fibration
with a section and
C_{i}\subset S
,
1\leq i\leq n
, are
n
distinct smooth fibers. The sky-scraper sheaf
{\mathbb{C}}_{p}
supported on the origin
p
of the lagrangian abelian variety

A:=\prod_{i=1}^{n}C_{i}\subset S^{[n]}
is the image of
{\mathcal{O}}_{A}
via an auto-equivalence of
D^{b}(S^{[n]})
.
We prove the following statement.

Theorem 1.1
.

(Theorem
6.24
)
Let
\iota:Z\rightarrow X
be an embedding of a variety
Z
as a proper subvariety of an irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold
X
. Let
F
be a torsion free coherent sheaf on
Z
. If
{\rm obs}_{\iota_{*}F}
has rank
1
, then
\iota(Z)
is either a lagrangian subvariety or a point.

1.1.3.
Deformability of sheaves with a rank
1
obstruction map

The following theorem is a consequence of Verbitsky’s results on hyperholomorphic sheaves in
[
Ve5
]
and the fact that
\kappa(F)
remains a Hodge class under all deformations for a sheaf
F
with a rank
1
obstruction homomorphism
{\rm obs}_{F}
.

Theorem 1.2
.

(Theorem

3.4
)
Let
F
be a reflexive sheaf of rank
r>0
over an irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold
X
. Assume that
{\rm obs}_{F}
has rank
1
and that
F
is
H
-slope-stable with respect to some
ample line-bundle
H
.
Then for every irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold
Y
deformation equivalent to
X
there exists a possibly twisted coherent sheaf
E
over
Y
and a flat
deformation of the pair
(X,F)
to the pair (Y,E).

Following is a sketch of the proof of Theorem
1.2
. Results of Verbitsky
in
[
Ve5
]

reduce the proof to showing that
c_{2}({\mathcal{E}}nd(F))
remains of Hodge type
(2,2)
under all deformations of
X
as a Kähler manifold.
The latter reduces to showing that
c_{2}({\mathcal{E}}nd(F))
remains of Hodge type
(2,2)
under all first order infinitesimal complex deformations of
X
, by Lemma
2.3
.
Set
HT^{2}(X):=H^{0}(X,\wedge^{2}TX)\oplus H^{1}(X,TX)\oplus H^{2}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X})
.
The Hochschild-Kostant-Rosenberg isomorphism

I^{HKR}:HT^{2}(X)\rightarrow HH^{2}(X)
composes with
{\rm obs}_{F}
to a rank
1
homomorphism

{\rm obs}^{HT}_{F}:={\rm obs}_{F}\circ I^{HKR}:HT^{2}(X)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{2}(F,F).

The class
c_{2}({\mathcal{E}}nd(F))
remains of Hodge type in the direction of
\xi\in H^{1}(X,TX)
, if and only if there exists a class

\theta\in H^{2}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X})
, such that
F
deforms to first order in the direction of
(0,\xi,\theta)\in HT^{2}(X)
. Toda proved that this is the case, if and only if
(0,\xi,\theta)\in HT^{2}(X)
belongs to the kernel of

{\rm obs}^{HT}_{F}
(here we use also a result of
[
Hua
]
that
{\rm obs}^{HT}_{F}
is the obstruction map used by Toda,
see Lemma
6.10
). Now, if the rank of
F
does not vanish, then

{\rm obs}^{HT}_{F}
maps
H^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{X})
injectively into
{\rm Ext}^{2}(F,F)
.
Thus, the intersection of
\ker({\rm obs}^{HT}_{F})
and
H^{1}(X,TX)\oplus H^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{X})

must project onto
H^{1}(X,TX)
. Hence, every
\xi\in H^{1}(X,TX)
lifts to a class
(0,\xi,\theta)
in
\ker({\rm obs}^{HT}_{F})
.

Remark 1.3
.

Theorem
1.2
holds also for abelian surfaces (Remark
3.5
). If
F
is an object in the derived category
D^{b}(X)
of an abelian variety
X
of dimension
\geq 3
, then the minimal rank of
{\rm obs}_{F}
is
h^{2,0}(X):=\dim(H^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}))
. Slope-stable vector bundles
F
with
{\rm obs}_{F}
of rank
h^{2,0}(X)
are semi-homogeneous (see Lemma
3.6
). These were extensively studied by Mukai
[
Mu3
]
.

1.2.
Examples of very modular vector bundles

Theorems
1.4
and
1.6
below exhibit two examples of sheaves
F
satisfying the conditions of the above Theorem.

1.2.1.
Very modular
{\mathbb{P}}^{n}
-objects over IHSMs of
K3^{[n]}
-type

Let
(S,H)
be a generic polarized
K3
surface, let
G
be an
H
-slope-stable locally free sheaf on
S
with vanishing

{\rm Ext}^{1}(G,G)
. Let the symmetric group
{\mathfrak{S}}_{n}
act on
S^{n}
permuting the factors.
The Hilbert scheme
S^{[n]}
of length
n
zero dimensional subschemes of
S
is isomorphic to an irreducible component of the
{\mathfrak{S}}_{n}
-Hilbert scheme of length
n!
zero-dimensional
{\mathfrak{S}}_{n}
-invariant subschemes
Z
of
S^{n}
, such that
H^{0}({\mathcal{O}}_{Z})
is the regular representation of
{\mathfrak{S}}_{n}

[
Hai
]
. There exists a universal

{\mathfrak{S}}_{n}
-invariant subscheme
\Gamma\subset S^{[n]}\times S^{n}
with a
{\mathfrak{S}}_{n}
-linearization
\rho
, such that the object
({\mathcal{O}}_{\Gamma},\rho)
is the Fourier-Mukai kernel of an equivalence

BKR:D^{b}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{n}}(S^{n})\rightarrow D^{b}(S^{[n]})
between the

{\mathfrak{S}}_{n}
-equivariant derived category of
S^{n}
and the derived category of
S^{[n]}

[
BKR
]
.
The vector bundle
G^{\boxtimes n}:=G\boxtimes G\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes G
over
S^{[n]}
comes with a canonical
{\mathfrak{S}}_{n}
-linearizarion
\rho_{\boxtimes}
.
The object
G^{[n]}:=BKR((G^{\boxtimes n},\rho_{\boxtimes}))
is isomorphic to a rank
r^{n}
locally free sheaf over
S^{[n]}
. Now,

{\rm Ext}^{2i}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{n}}((G^{\boxtimes n},\rho_{\boxtimes}),(G^{\boxtimes n},\rho_{\boxtimes}))

is
1
-dimensional, for
0\leq i\leq n
, hence
{\rm Ext}^{2i}(G^{[n]},G^{[n]})
is one-dimensional as well, for
0\leq i\leq n
, and so

{\rm obs}_{G^{[n]}}
has rank
1
.

Theorem 1.4
.

For every irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold
X
of
K3^{[n]}
deformation type there exists a possibly twisted locally free sheaf
E
and a deformation of the pair
(S^{[n]},G^{[n]})
to
(X,E)
.
E
is a
{\mathbb{P}}^{n}
-object, i.e.,
{\rm Ext}^{i}(E,E)\cong H^{i}({\mathbb{P}}^{n},{\mathbb{C}})
, and in particular
E
is
infinitesimally rigid.
E
is slope-stable with respect to classes in an open subcone of the Kähler cone of
X
.

The theorem is proved in section
10
.
The theorem was proved by O’Grady in case
n=2
, where it was further proven that the moduli space of vector bundles with the same Chern classes as
E
is a single point
[
O2
]
.
O’Grady proved that the Chern class
c_{2}({\mathcal{E}}nd(G^{[2]}))
remains of Hodge type under all Kähler deformations of
S^{[2]}
by explicitly computing this class.
The current paper emerged from the author’s attempt to provide a conceptual explanation to O’Grady’s intriguing result.

Remark 1.5
.

The pair
(S,G)
in Theorem
1.4
is related, via a finite sequence
of deformations and derived equivalences, to the pair
(T,{\mathcal{O}}_{T})
, for some
K3
surface
T
.
More precisely, there are two sequences
(S_{i},G_{i})
,
0\leq i\leq N
, and
(S^{\prime}_{i},G^{\prime}_{i})
,
0\leq i\leq N-1
,
and derived equivalences
\Phi_{i}:D^{b}(S^{\prime}_{i})\rightarrow D^{b}(S_{i+1})
,
0\leq i\leq N-1
, such that

(S_{0},G_{0})=(S,G)
,
(S_{N},G_{N})=(T,{\mathcal{O}}_{T})
,

(S^{\prime}_{i},G^{\prime}_{i})
is a deformation of
(S_{i},G_{i})
, and
G_{i+1}
is a slope-stable locally free sheaf isomorphic to
\Phi_{i}(G^{\prime}_{i})
.
Now,
{\mathcal{O}}_{T}^{[n]}={\mathcal{O}}_{T^{[n]}}
and the BKR-conjugate
\Phi_{i}^{[n]}:D^{b}((S^{\prime}_{i})^{[n]})\rightarrow D^{b}(S_{i+1}^{[n]})
of the derived equivalence

\Phi_{i}^{\boxtimes n}:D^{b}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{n}}((S^{\prime}_{i})^{n})\cong D^{b}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{n}}(S_{i+1}^{n})
is an equivalence mapping

(G^{\prime}_{i})^{[n]}
to
G_{i+1}^{[n]}
.
Hence, the pair
(S^{[n]},G^{[n]})
is related to the pair
(T^{[n]},{\mathcal{O}}_{T^{[n]}})
via a sequence of deformations and derived equivalences.

1.2.2.
A very modular vector bundle on
S^{[n]}
from
n
vector bundles on
S

Let
(S,H)
be a polarized
K3
surface and
M_{H}(v)
a
2
-dimensional projective moduli space of

H
-slope-stable locally free sheaves over
S
with Mukai vector
v
of rank
r
. Then
M_{H}(v)
is a
K3
surface

[
Mu2
]
.
Let
{\mathcal{U}}
be a universal vector bundle over
S\times M_{H}(v)
, possibly twisted by a Brauer class
\theta
.
Then the Fourier-Mukai transformation
\Phi_{\mathcal{U}}:D^{b}(M_{H}(v),\theta^{-1})\rightarrow D^{b}(S)
is an equivalence
[
Ca4
, Theorem 1.3]
.
Choose
n
distinct points
[G_{i}]
in
M_{H}(v)
corresponding to
n
vector bundles
G_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq n
.
The vector bundle
G:=\oplus_{\sigma\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{n}}G_{\sigma(1)}\boxtimes G_{\sigma(2)}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes G_{\sigma(n)}

admits a
{\mathfrak{S}}_{n}
-linearization
\rho
and any two linearizations yield isomorphic objects
(G,\rho)
(Remark
4.1
).
The object
BKR(G,\rho)
is isomorphic to a rank
(n!)r^{n}
locally free sheaf
F
over
S^{[n]}
.
Let
z\in(M_{H}(v))^{[n]}
be the point corresponding to the length
n
subscheme of
M_{H}(v)
supported on the set

\{[G_{i}]\ :\ 1\leq i\leq n\}.
Let
\Phi_{\mathcal{U}}^{[n]}:D^{b}(M_{H}(v))^{[n]},(\theta^{-1})^{[n]})\rightarrow D^{b}(S^{[n]})
be the BKR conjugate of

\Phi_{\mathcal{U}}^{\boxtimes n}:D^{b}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{n}}((M_{H}(v))^{n},(\theta^{-1})^{\boxtimes n})\rightarrow D^{b}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{n}}(S^{n})
.

F
is isomorphic to the image
\Phi_{\mathcal{U}}^{[n]}({\mathbb{C}}_{z})
of the sky-scraper sheaf
{\mathbb{C}}_{z}
(see Eq. (
11.7
) for details). Hence,

{\rm obs}_{F}
has rank
1
.

Theorem 1.6
.

For every irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold
X
of
K3^{[n]}
deformation type there exists a possibly twisted locally free sheaf
E
and a deformation of the pair
(S^{[n]},F)
to
(X,E)
.
E
is slope-stable with respect to classes in an open subcone of the Kähler cone of
X
.

The theorem is proved in Section
11
.
A criterion for the existence of an untwisted sheaf
E
in Theorems
1.4
and
1.6
is provided in Section
3.3
.

1.3.
The LLV line of an object with a rank
1
obstruction map

1.3.1.
The LLV-line of an object
F
with
{\rm rank}({\rm obs}_{F})=1

We do not know if the property that
{\rm obs}_{F}
has rank
1
is an open condition. A priori, the rank of
{\rm obs}_{F}
may increase after a deformation of
(X,F)
. However, the following theorem lists topological consequences of the fact that the rank of
{\rm obs}_{F}
is one, and those are clearly deformation invariant.

Let
X
be a
2n
-dimensional irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold.
Consider the vector space

\widetilde{H}(X,{\mathbb{Q}}):={\mathbb{Q}}\alpha\oplus H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})\oplus{\mathbb{Q}}\beta,

endowed with the symmetric bilinear pairing with respect to which
\alpha
and
\beta
are isotropic,
(\alpha,\beta)=-1
,
U:={\rm span}\{\alpha,\beta\}

is orthogonal to
H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
, and the pairing restricts to the Beauville-Bogomolov-Fujiki pairing on
H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
.
Verbitsky and Looijenga-Lunts constructed a canonical (topological) representation of the
Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{s}o}(\widetilde{H}(X,{\mathbb{Q}}))
on the cohomology
H^{*}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
(see
[
LL
,
Ve3
]
). Denote by
{\mathfrak{g}}_{X}
the image of
{\mathfrak{s}o}(\widetilde{H}(X,{\mathbb{Q}}))
in

{\mathfrak{g}l}(H^{*}(X,{\mathbb{Q}}))
. The subring
SH^{*}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
of
H^{*}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
generated by
H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
is isomorphic
as a
{\mathfrak{g}}_{X}
-subrepresentation to the irreducible subrepresentation
V(n)

of
{\rm Sym}^{n}(\widetilde{H}(X,{\mathbb{Q}}))
spanned by
n
-th powers of isotropic classes and
V(n)
appears with multiplicity
1
in
H^{*}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
. Hence, we get a canonical projection from
H^{*}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
to
SH^{*}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
.
Given an equivalence
\Phi:D^{b}(X)\rightarrow D^{b}(Y)
between two projective irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifolds
we get an induced isomorphism
\phi:H^{*}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})\rightarrow H^{*}(Y,{\mathbb{Q}})
.
Taelman proved that
\phi
conjugates
{\mathfrak{g}}_{X}
to
{\mathfrak{g}}_{Y}
and the restriction of
\phi

to
SH^{*}(X)
is induced by an isometry

\tilde{\phi}:\widetilde{H}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})\rightarrow\widetilde{H}(Y,{\mathbb{Q}})
.
Furthermore, there exists a canonical

{\mathfrak{g}}_{X}
-equivariant isomorphism

\Psi:SH^{*}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})\rightarrow V(n),

which is
(\phi,\tilde{\phi})
-equivariant, up to sign
[
Ta
, Theorems 4.7 and 4.9]
.
Given an object
F
of
D^{b}(X)
, let
v(F):=ch(F)\sqrt{td_{X}}
be its Mukai vector and let
\hat{v}(F)
be the projection of
v(F)
to
SH^{*}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
. Note that
\hat{v}(F)
does not vanish, if
{\rm rank}(F)\neq 0
or if
F
is a sky scraper sheaf, or if
F
is the image of such an object via an equivalence of derived categories.

Theorem 1.7
.

(Theorem
6.13
)
Let
F
be an object of
D^{b}(X)
with
{\rm obs}_{F}
of rank
1
. Assume that
\hat{v}(F)
does not vanish.

(1)

The stabilizer
{\mathfrak{g}}_{v(F)}
of
v(F)
in
{\mathfrak{g}}_{X}
is equal to the subalgebra of
{\mathfrak{g}}_{X}

annihilating a line
\ell(F)
in
\widetilde{H}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
.

(2)

The isomorphism
\Psi
maps
\hat{v}(F)
into the image of
\ell(F)^{n}
via the projection from
{\rm Sym}^{n}(\widetilde{H}(X,{\mathbb{Q}}))
to
V(n)
.

(3)

If the rank
r
of
F
does not vanish, then

\ell(F)={\rm span}\{r\alpha+c_{1}(F)+s\beta\}
, for some rational number
s
.

(4)

Given an equivalence
\Phi:D^{b}(X)\rightarrow D^{b}(Y)
between two irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifolds,
we have
\ell(\Phi(F))=\tilde{\phi}(\ell(F))
.

Theorem
1.7
shows that

\widetilde{H}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
plays a role analogous to that of the Mukai lattice of a
K3
surface in the classification of objects
F
with
{\rm obs}_{F}
of rank
1
. Note that the rank of
{\rm obs}_{F}
is
1
for every simple sheaf
F
over a
K3
surface, since
Ext^{2}(F,F)
is
1
-dimensional. We refer to
\widetilde{H}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
as the
rational LLV lattice
. When
X
is of
K3^{[2]}
-type
2
2

2

The author was notified by Thorsten Beckmann that he extended
[
Ta
, Theorem 9.8]
for every manifold of
K3^{[n]}
-type, for all
n\geq 1
. Partial results were obtained independently by the author and are included in this paper (Remark
8.2
).

there exists an integral lattice in
\widetilde{H}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
, which is preserved by equivalences of derived categories and by monodromy operators
[
Ta
, Theorem 9.8]
.

The proof of Theorem
1.7
proceeds as follows. The Hodge decomposition exhibits
H^{*}(X,{\mathbb{C}})

as a module over the ring
HT^{*}(X):=\oplus_{a,b}H^{a}(X,\wedge^{b}TX)
. The action homomorphism

m:HT^{*}(X)\rightarrow{\mathfrak{g}l}(H^{*}(X,{\mathbb{C}}))
maps
HT^{2}(X)
into the LLV Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathbb{C}}:={\mathfrak{g}}_{X}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}{\mathbb{C}},
by
[
Ve1
]
(see also
[
Ta
, Prop. 2.9]
).
Let
\tilde{\Sigma}(F)
be the image of
\ker\left({\rm obs}^{HT}_{F}\right)
via the graded Duflo automorphism of
HT^{*}(X)
given by contraction with
\sqrt{td_{X}}
.
Then
m:HT^{2}(X)\rightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathbb{C}}
maps
\tilde{\Sigma}(F)
into the stabilizer
{\mathfrak{g}}_{{\mathbb{C}},v(F)}
in
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathbb{C}}
of the Mukai vector
v(F)
of
F
(see
[
Hua
]
, or Lemma

6.10
). We prove that
m(\tilde{\Sigma}(F))
and its complex conjugate
generate
{\mathfrak{g}}_{{\mathbb{C}},v(F)}
, if the rank of
{\rm obs}_{F}
is
1
(Lemma
6.7
).
Composing
m
with the action of
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathbb{C}}
on
\widetilde{H}(X,{\mathbb{C}})
yields
a homomorphism from
HT^{2}(X)
into
{\mathfrak{g}l}(\widetilde{H}(X,{\mathbb{C}}))
. Evaluation at
a holomorphic
2
-form on
X
, considered as an element of
\widetilde{H}(X,{\mathbb{C}})
, gives rise to a homomorphism

\mu:HT^{2}(X)\rightarrow\widetilde{H}(X,{\mathbb{C}})
which pulls back the pairing on
\widetilde{H}(X,{\mathbb{C}})
to one on
HT^{2}(X)
, canonical up to a scalar (Lemma
6.3
). We get the line
\tilde{\Sigma}(F)^{\perp}
in
HT^{2}(X)
orthogonal to
\tilde{\Sigma}(F)

and we set
\ell(F):=\mu\left(\tilde{\Sigma}(F)^{\perp}\right)
.
We show that

\ell(F)
is defined over
{\mathbb{Q}}
and

{\mathfrak{g}}_{{\mathbb{C}},v(F)}
is the complexification of the subalgebra of
{\mathfrak{g}}_{X}

annihilating
\ell(F)
(Lemma
6.9
).

1.3.2.
Computation of LLV-lines

The integral
\int_{X}\sqrt{td_{X}}
for
X
of
K3^{[n]}
and generalized Kummer deformation types was computed in
[
Sa
, Prop. 19 and 21]
. We use Sawon’s computations to show that

\ell({\mathcal{O}}_{X})={\rm span}\{4\alpha+(n+3)\beta\}
, if
X
is of
K3^{[n]}
deformation type,
and
\ell({\mathcal{O}}_{X})={\rm span}\{4\alpha+(n+1)\beta\}
, if
X
is of generalized Kummer deformation type (Lemma

6.18
).

It is sometimes challenging to prove that
{\rm obs}_{F}
has rank
1
, yet possible to prove
the property in Theorem
1.7
(
1
). We prove it in Lemma
7.3
for
the structure sheaf
{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}
of a smooth lagrangian surface
Z
in
X
of
K3^{[2]}
-type, when the restriction homomorphism
H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})\rightarrow H^{2}(Z,{\mathbb{Q}})
has rank
1
and
c_{1}(\omega_{Z})
belongs to its image.
If
F
satisfies the property in Theorem
1.7
(
1
)
we say that

F

has a rank
1
cohomological obstruction map

(see Definition
6.11
(
2
)).
The images of such objects
F
via derived equivalences
each has a rank
1
cohomological obstruction map
as well.
The modularity Theorem
1.2
above holds if we replace the property that
{\rm obs}_{F}
has rank
1
by the property that

F
has a rank
1
cohomological obstruction map.
The line
\ell({\mathcal{O}}_{Z})
of the structure sheaf of a lagrangian submanifold
Z
of
X
, with

{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}
having a rank
1
cohomological obstruction map,
is computed in Lemma
6.25
when
c_{1}(\omega_{Z})
belongs to the image of
the pullback homomorphism
\iota^{*}:H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})\rightarrow H^{2}(Z,{\mathbb{Q}})
via the embedding
\iota:Z\rightarrow X
.
The homomorphism
\iota^{*}
necessarily has rank
1
in this case and we have

\ell(\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z})={\rm span}\{\lambda+\frac{t(\lambda,\lambda)}{2}\beta\},

where
c_{1}(\omega_{Z})=t\iota^{*}(\lambda)
and
\lambda^{\perp}=\ker(\iota^{*})
(Lemma

6.25
).

In Section
8
we include a conjectural discussion of the following.

Question 1.8
.

Let
X
be a projective irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold.
Which lines in
\widetilde{H}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
are of the form
\ell(F)
, for some object
F

which has a rank
1
cohomological obstruction map?

Very little is known even for
X
of
K3^{[2]}
-deformation type. The question is related to the existence of lagrangian surfaces
in the generic polarized such
X
. When
\iota:Z\rightarrow X
is an embedding of a smooth lagrangian surface,

\iota^{*}:H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})\rightarrow H^{2}(Z,{\mathbb{Q}})
has rank
1
, and
c_{1}(\omega_{Z})
belongs to its image, then

\iota_{*}\iota^{*}L
has a rank
1
cohomological obstruction map
for every line bundle
L
on
X
,
by Lemma
7.3
.
We show that for a primitive polarization
L
, the existence of such a lagrangian surface, which locally deform with all deformations of
(X,L)
, implies that the Beauville-Bogomolov-Fujiki self-intersection
2d:=(c_{1}(L),c_{1}(L))
of
L
satisfies the following arithmetic constraint:
d/\gcd(3,d)

is a perfect square.

See Lemma
7.6
for a more detailed statement involving stronger arithmetic constraints.

1.4.
The action of an auto-equivalence of a
K3
surface
S
on the LLV-lattice of
S^{[n]}

Equivalences of derived categories of irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifolds of
K3^{[n]}
deformation type are morphisms of a groupoid
K3^{[n]}
, i.e., a category all of whose morphisms are isomorphisms. We have the functor
{}^{[n]}:K3^{[1]}\rightarrow K3^{[n]}
, sending a
K3
surface
S
to its Hilbert scheme
S^{[n]}
and sending equivalences of derived categories

\Phi:S_{1}\rightarrow S_{2}
to the equivalences
\Phi^{[n]}:D^{b}(S_{1}^{[n]})\rightarrow D^{b}(S_{2}^{[n]})
as in Theorems
1.4
and
1.6
above (see Section
4
for the definition).
Let
{LLV}
be the groupoid, whose objects are irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifolds and whose morphisms are isometries of their rational LLV lattices. Taelman’s work gives rise to a functor
\widetilde{H}:K3^{[n]}\rightarrow{LLV}
.
The values of the composite functor
\widetilde{H}\circ^{[n]}
on morphisms are not equal to those one may naively expect.
The composition
\widetilde{H}(\Phi^{[n]}):\widetilde{H}(S_{1}^{[n]},{\mathbb{Q}})\rightarrow\widetilde{H}(S_{2}^{[n]},{\mathbb{Q}})
was computed for every equivalence
\Phi:D^{b}(S_{1})\rightarrow D^{b}(S_{2})
of derived categories of
K3
surfaces and
for
n=2
in
[
Ta
, Theorem 9.4]
. We extend the computation for all
n
in the following theorem as an application of Theorem
1.7
above.

The space
H^{2}(S,{\mathbb{Q}})
is naturally the subspace
of
H^{2}(S^{[n]},{\mathbb{Q}})
orthogonal to the class
\delta
, which is half the class of the divisor of non-reduced subschemes.
The inclusion
H^{2}(S,{\mathbb{Q}})\subset H^{2}(S^{[n]},{\mathbb{Q}})

extends to a natural isometric embedding of
\widetilde{H}(S,{\mathbb{Q}})
onto
the hyperplane
\delta^{\perp}
in
\widetilde{H}(S^{[n]},{\mathbb{Q}})
orthogonal to the class
\delta
.
Let
\eta_{\Phi}\in O(\widetilde{H}(S^{[n]},{\mathbb{Q}}))
be the extension of
\widetilde{H}(\Phi)
leaving
\delta
invariant.
It turns out that
\widetilde{H}(\Phi^{[n]})
is not
equal to
\eta_{\Phi}
in general.
Associated to a class
\lambda\in H^{2}(S^{[n]},{\mathbb{Q}})
is an isometry
B_{\lambda}
of
\widetilde{H}(S^{[n]},{\mathbb{Q}})
,
which corresponds to the action of tensorization by a line bundle
L
whenever
\lambda=c_{1}(L)
.

Theorem 1.9
.

(Theorem
12.2
)

\widetilde{H}(\Phi^{[n]})=\det\left(\widetilde{H}(\Phi)\right)^{n+1}(B_{-\delta/2}\circ\eta_{\Phi}\circ B_{\delta/2})
.

Let
\chi
be the sign character of
{\mathfrak{S}}_{n}
and
let
\Phi_{\chi}^{[n]}:D^{b}(S^{[n]})\rightarrow D^{b}(S^{[n]})
be the
BKR
-conjugate of the auto-equivalence of

D^{b}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{n}}(S^{n})
of tensorization by the sign character. The proof of Theorem
1.9
is based on the observation that the isometries
\widetilde{H}(\Phi^{[n]})
all commute with

\widetilde{H}(\Phi_{\chi}^{[n]})
, and
\widetilde{H}(\Phi_{\chi}^{[n]})
is, up to sign, the reflection of
\widetilde{H}(S^{[n]},{\mathbb{Q}})

with respect to the hyperplane orthogonal to the class
B_{-\delta/2}(\delta)=\delta+(n-1)\beta

(Lemma
12.1
).

1.5.
A reflexive sheaf with a positive LLV-line

The vector bundle
E
in Theorem
1.4
has a negative definite LLV line
\ell(E)
and that in Theorem
1.6
has an isotropic LLV line.
In Section
13
we consider a particular example of a reflexive torsion free sheaf
E
, which
has a rank
1
cohomological obstruction map
and with a positive LLV line
\ell(E)
.
Let
X
be a cubic fourfold. The Fano variety of lines
F(X)
on
X
is holomorphic symplectic of
K3^{[2]}
-type, by
[
BD
]
. The Fano variety
F(C)
of lines on a hyperplane section
C
of
X
is a lagrangian surface in
F(X)

and
{\mathcal{O}}_{F(C)}
has a rank
1
cohomological obstruction map,
by Lemma
7.3
.
We exhibit reflexive torsion free sheaves
E
, which are Fourier-Mukai images of

{\mathcal{O}}_{F(C)}
. Each

E
thus has a rank
1
cohomological obstruction map.
We expect
E
to be slope-stable with respect to classes in an open subcone of the ample cone of
F(X)
. Once proven,
E
would be very modular, by Theorem
1.2
. Results of

[
LSV
,
Vo2
]
suggest
that the irreducible component
M
containing
E
of the moduli space of slope-stable sheaves on
F(X)
is birational to an irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold
of O’Grady10 deformation type. A possibly naive hope is that this would lead to a description of irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifolds
M
of O’Grady10 deformation type as moduli spaces of hyperholomorphic sheaves on holomorphic symplectic manifolds
Y
of
K3^{[2]}
-deformation type. A program of constructing irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifolds as such moduli spaces is described in
[
Ve5
]
. Such a modular description would enable the study of the cohomology ring of
M
via a universal sheaf on
Y\times M
. A modular description is currently provided only for the complement of an exceptional divisor of a resolution
M
of singularities of a moduli space of sheaves on a
K3
surface
[
O3
]
.

We refer the reader to the table of content for the organization of the paper. A summary of each section is included in the opening paragraph of the section.

2.
Real
(p,p)
-classes which remain of Hodge type to first order in all directions

In Section
2.1

we review the action of the Looijenga-Lunts-Verbitsky Lie algebra on the cohomology of an irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold
X
. In Section
2.2
we prove that if a real cohomology class of Hodge type
(p,p)
on such
X
remains a
(p,p)
class under all first order classical deformations of
X
, then it remains a
(p,p)
class under all Kähler deformations of
X
.

2.1.
The rational LLV lattice

Let
U
be the even unimodular rank
2
lattice with basis
\{\alpha,\beta\}
consisting of isotropic elements satisfying
(\alpha,\beta)=-1
. Set
U_{\mathbb{Q}}:=U\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
.
Following Taelman
[
Ta
, Sec. 3.1]
we define
the
rational LLV lattice
3
3

3

Taelman calls this vector space the
Rational Mukai lattice
. We prefer to distinguish it from Căldăraru’s Mukai lattice defined in
[
Ca2
,
Ca3
]
. LLV is short for Looijenga-Lunts-Verbitsky, as this vector space first appears in their work
[
LL
,
Ve3
]

of a
2n
-dimensional irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold
X
to be the orthogonal direct sum

(2.1)

\tilde{H}(X,{\mathbb{Q}}):=H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})\oplus U_{\mathbb{Q}},

where the bilinear pairing on the first direct summand is the Beauville-Bogomolov-Fujiki (BBF) pairing normalized so that

\int_{X}\lambda^{2n}=c_{X}\frac{(2n)!}{2^{n}n!}(\lambda,\lambda)^{n},\ \ \ \forall\lambda\in H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Q}}),

where the Fujiki constant
c_{X}
is a positive rational number such that the pairing
(\bullet,\bullet)
is integral, primitive, and positive on Kähler classes
[
O4
, Sec. 2.1]
. The Fujiki constant is calculated for all known irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifolds in
[
Ra
]
.
If
X
is of
K3^{[n]}
-type, then
c_{X}=1
, and
if
X
is a deformation equivalent to a generalized Kummer of dimension
2n
, then
c_{X}=n+1
.
We endow
\tilde{H}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
with a weight
0
Hodge structure extending that of
H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Q}}(1))
so that classes in
U_{\mathbb{Q}}
are of type
(0,0)
.

Set
{\mathfrak{g}}:={\mathfrak{s}o}(\tilde{H}(X,{\mathbb{Q}}))
. Let
\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}
be the subalgebra
{\mathfrak{s}o}(H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Q}}))
of
{\mathfrak{g}}
annihilating
U_{\mathbb{Q}}
.
Given a class
\lambda\in H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
denote by
e_{\lambda}
the element of
{\mathfrak{g}}
given by

(2.2)

e_{\lambda}(\alpha)=\lambda,\ \ e_{\lambda}(\lambda^{\prime})=(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})\beta,\ \forall\lambda^{\prime}\in H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Q}}),\ \mbox{and}\ \ e_{\lambda}(\beta)=0.

Example 2.1
.

Let
X
be the Hilbert scheme
S^{[n]}
of length
n
subschemes of a
K3
surface
S
. Let
\pi:S^{[n]}\rightarrow S^{(n)}
be the Hilbert Chow morphism. The composition of the isomorphism
H^{2}(S,{\mathbb{Z}})\cong H^{2}(S^{n},{\mathbb{Z}})^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{n}}\cong H^{2}(S^{(n)},{\mathbb{Z}})
with
\pi^{*}:H^{2}(S^{(n)},{\mathbb{Z}})\rightarrow H^{2}(S^{[n]},{\mathbb{Z}})
induces an isometric embedding

\iota
of
H^{2}(S,{\mathbb{Z}})
into
H^{2}(S^{[n]},{\mathbb{Z}})

[
Be1
]
.
Hence, there exists a unique extension of
\iota
to an isometric embedding, denoted by
\iota
as well, of
\tilde{H}(S,{\mathbb{Q}})
into
\tilde{H}(S^{[n]},{\mathbb{Q}})
satisfying

\iota\circ e_{\lambda}=e_{\iota(\lambda)}\circ\iota
, for all
\lambda\in H^{2}(S,{\mathbb{Z}})
.
It satisfies

\iota(\alpha)=\alpha
and
\iota(\beta)=\beta
.

Let
h:H^{*}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})\rightarrow H^{*}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
be the degree operator acting by
k-\dim_{\mathbb{C}}(X)
on
H^{k}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
. Given a class
\omega\in H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
, denote by
L_{\omega}:H^{*}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})\rightarrow H^{*}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
the operator of cup product with
\omega
.
If
\omega
is a Kähler class then there exists an operator
\Lambda_{\omega}:H^{*}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})\rightarrow H^{*}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
,
such that the triple
(L_{\omega},h,\Lambda_{\omega})
spans a Lie subalgebra of
{\mathfrak{g}l}(H^{*}(X,{\mathbb{Q}}))
isomorphic to
{\mathfrak{s}l}_{2}
, by the Hard-Lefschetz Theorem. Explicitly, we have

[L_{w},\Lambda_{w}]=h,\ [L_{w},h]=2L_{w},\ \mbox{and}\ [\Lambda_{w},h]=-2\Lambda_{w}.

We say that
(L_{\omega},h,\Lambda_{\omega})
is an
{\mathfrak{s}l}_{2}
-triple
with respect to
h
.
The set of classes
\omega\in H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
, for which there exists a necessarily unique operator
\Lambda_{w}
satisfying the above relations, is a Zariski dense subset of
H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
, by the Jacobson-Morozov Theorem.

Theorem 2.2
.

(
[
LL
, Prop. 4.5]
and
[
Ve3
]
)
There exists a unique isomorphism between the
Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}:={\mathfrak{s}o}(\tilde{H}(X,{\mathbb{Q}}))
and the subalgebra of
{\mathfrak{g}l}(H^{*}(X,{\mathbb{Q}}))
generated by all
{\mathfrak{s}l}_{2}
-triples
(L_{\omega},h,\Lambda_{\omega})
,
\omega\in H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
,
which sends
e_{\lambda}
to
L_{\lambda}
. The subalgebra of
{\mathfrak{g}}
preserving the grading of
H^{*}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
is
\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}\oplus{\mathbb{Q}}h
.

The isomorphism in the above theorem is proved by Verbitsky and Looijenga-Luntz over
{\mathbb{R}}
, but their proof goes through over
{\mathbb{Q}}

[
GKLR
, Theorem 2.7]
.

2.2.
Real
(p,p)
-classes

Let
X
be an irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold.

Lemma 2.3
.

Let
\alpha\in H^{p,p}(X,{\mathbb{R}})
be a real class of Hodge type
(p,p)
. Assume that
\alpha
remains of Hodge type under all first order deformations of
X
as a complex manifold. Then
\alpha
remains of Hodge type under every Kähler deformation of
X
.

Proof.

Let
I
be the complex structure of
X
and let

(2.3)

f_{I}:H^{*}(X,{\mathbb{R}})\rightarrow H^{*}(X,{\mathbb{R}})

be the Hodge operator acting on
H^{p,q}(X,I)
by

(q-p)\sqrt{-1}
. Then
f_{I}
belongs to
\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{\mathbb{R}}:=\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}{\mathbb{R}}
, by
[
Ve1
, Theorem 8.1]
.
Every Kähler deformation of an irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold is an irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold

[
Be1
]
.
Hence, if the class
\alpha
is
\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}
-invariant, then it remains of Hodge type under every Kähler deformation.
We prove the
\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}
-invariance of
\alpha
in two steps.

Step 1:

The class
\alpha
belongs to the kernel of all homomorphisms in the image of the natural homomorphism

H^{1}(X,TX)\rightarrow{\rm Hom}(H^{p,p}(X),H^{p-1,p+1}(X)),

since
\alpha
remains of Hodge type under all first order deformations.
The above homomorphism is the restriction of the contraction homomorphism

\iota\ :\ H^{1}(X,TX)\rightarrow{\rm Hom}(H^{*}(X,{\mathbb{C}}),H^{*}(X,{\mathbb{C}})),

where
\iota_{\eta}
,
\eta\in H^{1}(X,TX)
, maps
H^{p,q}(X)
to
H^{p-1,q+1}(X)
.
The image of the homomorphism
\iota
is contained in the subalgebra
\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{\mathbb{C}}
, by
[
GKLR
, Lemma 4.5]
, so that
\iota
factors through a homomorphism

\tilde{\iota}\ :\ H^{1}(X,TX)\rightarrow\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{\mathbb{C}},

which we recall next
4
4

4

The homomorphism
\tilde{\iota}
is extended in Corollary
6.2
below to a homomorphism
HT^{2}(X)\rightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathbb{C}}
.

.
Fix a Kähler metric on
X
, let
I
be the complex structure on
X
and let
\omega_{I}:=g(I\bullet,\bullet)
be the fundamental form.
We get the twistor data consisting of complex structures
J
and
K
, compatible with
g
and satisfying the quaternionic relations

IJ=K
, as well as the Kähler forms
\omega_{J}:=g(J(\bullet),\bullet)
and
\omega_{K}:=g(K(\bullet),\bullet)
, such that

\sigma:=\omega_{J}+\sqrt{-1}\omega_{K}
is a global holomorphic
(2,0)
-form on
X
with respect to the complex structure
I

[
Be1
]
.
Let
L_{I}\in{\mathfrak{g}l}(H^{*}(X,{\mathbb{C}}))
be cup product with
\omega_{I}
, define
L_{J}
and
L_{K}
similarly, and let

\Lambda_{I}
,
\Lambda_{J}
, and
\Lambda_{K}
be the adjoint Lefschetz operators.
Set
\Lambda_{\sigma}:=\frac{1}{2}(\Lambda_{J}-\sqrt{-1}\Lambda_{K})
. Given
\eta\in H^{1}(X,TX)
, let
L_{\iota_{\eta}(\sigma)}
be cup product with the

(1,1)
-class
\iota_{\eta}(\sigma)
.
Then
\iota_{\eta}=[L_{\iota_{\eta}(\sigma)},\Lambda_{\sigma}]
and the latter belongs to
\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{\mathbb{C}}
, so that
\iota
factors through the homomorphism
\tilde{\iota}

sending
\eta\in H^{1}(X,TX)
to
[L_{\iota_{\eta}(\sigma)},\Lambda_{\sigma}]
, by
[
GKLR
, Lemma 4.5]
.

The Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{s}o}(H^{1,1}(X))
is the subspace of

{\rm Hom}[H^{1,1}(X),H^{1,1}(X)]
of anti-self-dual homomorphisms with respect to the BBF-pairing.
The one-dimensional Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{s}o}(H^{2,0}(X)\oplus H^{0,2}(X))
is characterized similarly.
Elements of
{\mathfrak{s}o}(H^{2,0}(X)\oplus H^{0,2}(X))
have traceless diagonal matrices in the basis
\{\sigma,\bar{\sigma}\}
.
The complex structure
I
induces a weight zero Hodge structure on
\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{\mathbb{C}}\cong{\mathfrak{s}o}(H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{C}}))
, the Hodge decomposition is
{\mathfrak{s}o}(H^{1,1}(X))
-invariant, and the Hodge direct summands are the following
{\mathfrak{s}o}(H^{1,1}(X))
-modules:

\displaystyle\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}^{-1,1}

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}(H^{2,0}(X),H^{1,1}(X)),

\displaystyle\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}^{0,0}

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{s}o}(H^{1,1}(X))\ \ \ \ \oplus\ \ \ {\mathfrak{s}o}(H^{2,0}(X)\oplus H^{0,2}(X))

\displaystyle\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}^{1,-1}

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}(H^{0,2}(X),H^{1,1}(X)).

Note that
{\rm Hom}(H^{2,0}(X),H^{1,1}(X))
is isomorphic to
{\rm Hom}(H^{1,1}(X),H^{0,2}(X))
via the BBF pairing and
\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}^{-1,1}
is embedded in the direct sum of the two. A similar remark holds for
\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}^{1,-1}
.
The image of
\iota
is equal to
\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}^{-1,1}
and so
\alpha
is infinitesimally invariant with respect to
\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}^{-1,1}
, i.e., annihilated by it.
The class
\alpha
is assumed to be real. Hence, it is invariant with respect to the complex conjugate subalgebra

\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}^{1,-1}
.
It remains to prove that the two Lie subalgebras
\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}^{-1,1}
and
\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}^{1,-1}
generate
\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{\mathbb{C}}
.

Step 2:

The Lie bracket induces the following homomorphism of
{\mathfrak{s}o}(H^{1,1}(X))
-modules:

(2.4)

\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}^{-1,1}\otimes\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}^{1,-1}\rightarrow\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}^{0,0}.

Normalize
\sigma
so that
(\sigma,\bar{\sigma})=1
.
We have

(\iota_{\eta_{1}}(\sigma),\iota_{\eta_{2}}(\sigma))=(\sigma,-\iota_{\eta_{1}}(\iota_{\eta_{2}}(\sigma))),

since
\iota_{\eta_{1}}
belongs to
\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{\mathbb{C}}
, and
\iota_{\eta_{1}}(\iota_{\eta_{2}}(\sigma))
is a scalar multiple of
\bar{\sigma}
. We conclude that

\iota_{\eta_{1}}(\iota_{\eta_{2}}(\sigma))=-(\iota_{\eta_{1}}(\sigma),\iota_{\eta_{2}}(\sigma))\bar{\sigma}.

Given
\eta\in H^{1}(TX)
, let
\bar{\iota_{\eta}}(\bullet):=\overline{\iota_{\eta}(\bar{\bullet})}
be the complex conjugate element.
Note that
\iota_{\eta}(\bar{\sigma})=0
and
\bar{\iota_{\eta}}(\sigma)=0.

The following equalities thus hold for all
\eta_{1},\eta_{2},\eta_{3}\in H^{1}(TX)
.

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt[\iota_{\eta_{1}},\bar{\iota_{\eta_{2}}}](\sigma)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\overline{\iota_{\eta_{2}}(\sigma)},\iota_{\eta_{1}}(\sigma))\sigma,

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt[\iota_{\eta_{1}},\bar{\iota_{\eta_{2}}}](\bar{\sigma})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(\iota_{\eta_{1}}(\sigma),\overline{\iota_{\eta_{2}}(\sigma)})\bar{\sigma},

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt[\iota_{\eta_{1}},\bar{\iota_{\eta_{2}}}](\iota_{\eta_{3}}(\sigma))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(\overline{\iota_{\eta_{2}}(\sigma)},\iota_{\eta_{3}}(\sigma))\iota_{\eta_{1}}(\sigma)+(\iota_{\eta_{1}}(\sigma),\iota_{\eta_{3}}(\sigma))\overline{\iota_{\eta_{2}}(\sigma)}.

Choose
\eta\in H^{1}(TX)
so that
\iota_{\eta}(\sigma)
is a non-isotropic real
(1,1)
class of self intersection
t
.
We see that
[\iota_{\eta},\bar{\iota_{\eta}}](\sigma)=t\sigma
,

[\iota_{\eta},\bar{\iota_{\eta}}](\bar{\sigma})=-t\bar{\sigma}
, and
[\iota_{\eta},\bar{\iota_{\eta}}]
annihilates
H^{1,1}(X)
.
Hence,
[\iota_{\eta},\bar{\iota_{\eta}}]
spans the trivial
{\mathfrak{s}o}(H^{1,1}(X))
submodule
{\mathfrak{s}o}({\rm Hom}[H^{2,0}(X)\oplus H^{0,2}(X))
, and so the latter is contained in the image of (
2.4
).

Choose non-zero elements
\eta_{1},\eta_{2}\in H^{1}(TX)
, so that each of
\omega_{i}:=\iota_{\eta_{i}}(\sigma)
,
i=1,2
, is real and

(\omega_{1},\omega_{2})=0.
Then

[\iota_{\eta_{1}},\bar{\iota_{\eta_{2}}}]
annihilates both
\sigma
and
\bar{\sigma}
and

[\iota_{\eta_{1}},\bar{\iota_{\eta_{2}}}](\omega)=-(\omega_{2},\omega)\omega_{1}+(\omega_{1},\omega)\omega_{2}
, for all
\omega\in H^{1,1}(X)
. Thus,
[\iota_{\eta_{1}},\bar{\iota_{\eta_{2}}}]
is a non-zero element of
{\mathfrak{s}o}(H^{1,1}(X))
.
Thus, the image of (
2.4
) must contain the irreducible
{\mathfrak{s}o}(H^{1,1}(X))
-submodule

{\mathfrak{s}o}(H^{1,1}(X))
as well.

∎

Remark 2.4
.

The analogue of Lemma
2.3
when
X
is a
n
-dimensional complex torus holds as well, except that the conclusion is that the real
(p,p)
class
\alpha
must vanish if
0<p<n
. The proof is completely analogous.
The LLV Lie algebra of a complex torus
X
is

{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathbb{R}}={\mathfrak{s}o}\left(H^{1}(X,{\mathbb{R}})\oplus H^{1}(X,{\mathbb{R}})^{*}\right),

where the pairing on
H^{1}(X,{\mathbb{R}})\oplus H^{1}(X,{\mathbb{R}})^{*}
is
((\alpha_{1},\theta_{1}),(\alpha_{2},\theta_{2}))=\theta_{1}(\alpha_{2})+\theta_{2}(\alpha_{1})
. The semi-simple direct summand of the reductive subalgebra of
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathbb{R}}
preserving the grading of
H^{*}(X,{\mathbb{R}})
is
\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{\mathbb{R}}={\mathfrak{s}l}(H^{1}(X,{\mathbb{R}}))
,
by
[
LL
, Prop. 3.2]
. The Hodge decomposition of
\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{\mathbb{C}}
is

\displaystyle\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}^{-1,1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}(H^{1,0}(X),H^{0,1}(X)),

\displaystyle\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}^{1,-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}(H^{0,1}(X),H^{1,0}(X)),

\displaystyle\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}^{0,0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ker\left[{\mathfrak{g}l}(H^{1,0}(X))\oplus{\mathfrak{g}l}(H^{0,1}(X))\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle tr+tr}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathbb{C}}.

Again,
\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}^{-1,1}
and
\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}^{1,-1}
generate
\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{\mathbb{C}}
. However, in this case

H^{i}(X,{\mathbb{C}})=\wedge^{i}H^{1}(X,{\mathbb{C}})
is an irreducible representation of
\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{\mathbb{C}}
and so every
\bar{{\mathfrak{g}}}_{\mathbb{C}}
-invariant
(p,p)
class vanishes, for
0<p<n
.

3.
Stable reflexive sheaves with a rank
1
obstruction map are very modular

In Section
3.1
we prove that stable reflexive sheaves with a rank
1
obstruction map are very modular (Theorem
3.4
).
In Section
3.2
we exhibit examples of coherent sheaves with a rank
1
obstruction map. Let
X
and
Y
be deformation equivalent irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifolds and
F
a stable reflexive coherent (untwisted) sheaf on
X
with
{\rm obs}_{F}
of rank
1
.
In Section
3.3
we provide a necessary and sufficient conditions for there to be a coherent (untwisted) sheaf
E
on
Y
, such that
(Y,E)
is related to
(X,F)
by a sequence of deformations and tensorization by line bundles (Lemma
3.13
). We then provide an easy to check sufficient condition in the
K3^{[n]}
and generalized kummer deformation types (Lemma
3.16
).

3.1.
Proof of modularity

Let
F
be a coherent, possibly twisted, sheaf of positive rank
r
.
We get the untwisted object
G:=F^{\otimes r}\otimes\det(F)^{*}
of rank
r^{r}
in the derived category of
X
and we let
\kappa(F)\in H^{*}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
be the
r
-th root of
ch(G)
with value
r
in
H^{0}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
, see

[
Ma4
, Sec. 2.2]
for a detailed definition.

An equivalence

\Phi:D^{b}(X)\rightarrow D^{b}(Y)
of derived categories
induces a graded isomorphism
\phi:HH^{*}(X)\rightarrow HH^{*}(Y)
of Hochschild cohomologies, by

[
Ca2
]
.
Set
HT^{k}(X):=\oplus_{p+q=k}H^{q}(X,\wedge^{p}TX)
.
We have the graded Hochschild-Kostant-Rosenberg isomorphism

I^{HKR}:HT^{*}(X)\rightarrow HH^{*}(X)
. The space
HT^{2}(X)=H^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{X})\oplus H^{1}(TX)\oplus H^{0}(\wedge^{2}TX)
parametrizes first order generalized deformations of the category of coherent sheaves on
X
, and also of
D^{b}(X)

[
To
]
.

The Atiyah class of an object
G
in
D^{b}(X)
is a class
a_{G}
in
{\rm Hom}(G,G\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X}[1])
(see
[
Ka
]
).
Let
a_{G,i}\in{\rm Hom}(G,G\otimes\Omega^{i}_{X}[i])
be the
i
power of
a_{G}
.
The exponential Atiyah class is

\exp a_{G}:=\oplus_{i\geq 0}a_{G,i}:G\rightarrow\oplus_{i\geq 0}(G\otimes\Omega_{X}^{i}[i]).

The contraction homomorphism

(3.1)

{\rm obs}^{HT}_{G}\ :\ HT^{2}(X)\ \rightarrow\ {\rm Hom}(G,G[2])

sends a class
\xi\in HT^{2}(X)
to
the obstruction class
\xi\cdot\exp a_{G}

to first order deformation of
G
in the direction
\xi

[
To
, Prop. 6.1]
.
Hence, if
{\rm Hom}(G,G[2])
is one dimensional,
then the kernel
\Sigma(G)\subset HT^{2}(X)
has co-dimension at most one.

Given an object
G
in the bounded derived category
D^{b}(X)
of a smooth projective variety
X
, denote by
\Sigma(G)\subset HT^{2}(X)
the kernel of (
3.1
). Note that

\Sigma({\mathcal{O}}_{X})
contains
H^{0}(\wedge^{2}TX)\oplus H^{1}(TX)
, by the proof of
[
To
, Lemma 4.3]
in which Toda constructs a first order deformation of
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
for each class in
H^{0}(\wedge^{2}TX)\oplus H^{1}(TX)
.
Given a closed point
z\in X
let
{\mathcal{O}}_{z}
be its sky-scraper sheaf and
I_{z}
its ideal sheaf.
Now,
{\rm Ext}^{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{z},{\mathcal{O}}_{z}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X})\cong H^{0}({\mathcal{E}}xt^{1}_{X}({\mathcal{O}}_{z},{\mathcal{O}}_{z}))\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X,z}\cong{\rm End}(T_{z}X),
the Atiyah class

a_{{\mathcal{O}}_{z}}
is the identity, and
a_{{\mathcal{O}}_{z},2}
is the identity endomorphism of
\wedge^{2}(T_{z}X)
.
Thus,

\Sigma({\mathcal{O}}_{z})=H^{2}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X})\oplus H^{1}(X,TX)\oplus H^{0}(X,(\wedge^{2}TX)\otimes I_{z}).

Hence, if
X
is an irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold, then both

\Sigma({\mathcal{O}}_{X})

and
\Sigma({\mathcal{O}}_{z})
have co-dimension
1
in
HH^{2}(X)
.

The co-dimension of
\Sigma(G)
is one also
for every object
G
over an irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold
Y
, which is isomorphic to the image

\Phi(F)
of an object
F
with
\Sigma(F)
of codimension one,
via an equivalence of derived categories
\Phi:D^{b}(X)\rightarrow D^{b}(Y)
,
by
[
To
, Theorem 4.7]
.

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
X
be a projective irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifolds and

F
an object in
D^{b}(X)
of positive rank. Assume that
the projection from
HT^{2}(X)
to its direct summand
H^{1}(TX)
maps the intersection

(3.2)

\Sigma(F)\ \ \ \cap\ \ \ \left[H^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{X})\oplus H^{1}(TX)\right]

onto
H^{1}(TX)
.
Then the class
\kappa(F)
remains of Hodge type under every Kähler deformation of
X
.

Proof.

Let
\beta
be an element of
H^{1}(TX)
. There exists a class
\alpha\in H^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{X})
, such that the class
(\alpha,\beta,0)\in HT^{2}(X)
belongs to
\Sigma(F)
, by assumption.
The object
F
deforms in the direction
(\alpha,\beta,0)
, by
[
To
, Prop. 6.1]
. More precisely, there exists a perfect complex
{\mathcal{F}}
(i.e., locally quasi-isomorphic to a bounded complex of free modules) of
\tilde{\alpha}
-twisted sheaves
in the category
Mod({\mathcal{O}}_{X}^{\beta},\tilde{\alpha})
constructed in

[
To
, Sec. 4]
, where
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}^{\beta}
is the structure sheaf of the first order deformation
{\mathcal{X}}
of
X
in the commutative direction
\beta
and
\tilde{\alpha}
is the natural lift of
\alpha
to a Brauer class in
H^{2}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{X}}}^{*})
. We get the rank
1
locally free
\tilde{\alpha}^{r}
-twisted
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{X}}}
-module
\det({\mathcal{F}})
over
{\mathcal{X}}
, by

[
KM
, Theorem 2]
, and
the untwisted deformation
\det({\mathcal{F}})^{*}\otimes({\mathcal{F}}^{\vee})^{\otimes r}
over
{\mathcal{X}}
of the object

E:=\det(F)^{*}\otimes(F^{\vee})^{\otimes r}
.
Hence, the class
ch(E)
remains of Hodge type in every direction
\beta\in H^{1}(TX)
.
Thus, so does its
r
-th root
\kappa(F)
. The statement now follows from Lemma
2.3
.
∎

Remark 3.2
.

Let
X
be a projective irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold and
F
an object of
D^{b}(X)
.
We include the case where the rank of
F
is zero.
If
H^{1}(TX)
is contained in the kernel
\Sigma(F)
of the obstruction map (
3.1
), then
ch(F)
remains of
Hodge type under every Kähler deformation of
X
, by the proof of Lemma
3.1
.

Proposition 3.3
.

Let
\Phi:D^{b}(X)\rightarrow D^{b}(Y)
be an equivalence of derived categories of coherent sheaves of two projective irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifolds. Assume that an object
G
in
D^{b}(X)

satisfies the following two conditions.

(1)

\Sigma(G)
has co-dimension
1
in
HT^{2}(X)
.

(2)

The rank of
F:=\Phi(G)
is positive.

Then the class
\kappa(F)
remains of Hodge type under every Kähler deformation of
Y.

Proof.

The equality
\Sigma(F)=\phi(\Sigma(G))
follows from
[
To
, Theorem 4.7]
.
Hence,
\Sigma(F)
has co-dimension
1
in
HT^{2}(Y)
, since

\Sigma(G)
is assumed to have co-dimension
1
in
HT^{2}(X)
.
We claim that the projection from
HT^{2}(Y)
to its direct summand
H^{1}(TY)
maps the intersection

\Sigma(F)\ \ \ \cap\ \ \ \left[H^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{Y})\oplus H^{1}(TY)\right]

onto
H^{1}(TY)
.
This surjectivity statement is equivalent to the condition that either the two hyperplanes
\Sigma(F)
and
H^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{Y})\oplus H^{1}(Y,TY)
are equal, or

H^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{Y})
is not contained in
\Sigma(F)
.
The obstruction map (
3.1
) is defined as
contraction with the exponential Atiyah class

exp\ a_{F}\in\oplus_{i\geq 1}{\rm Hom}(F,F\otimes\Omega_{Y}^{i}[i]),

[
To
, Def. 5.5]
.
The direct summand of the latter in
{\rm Hom}(F,F\otimes\Omega_{Y}^{0}[0])={\rm Hom}(F,F)
is the identity.
Hence, (
3.1
) restricts to
H^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{Y})

as the homomorphism
u:{\rm Hom}({\mathcal{O}}_{Y},{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}[2])\rightarrow{\rm Hom}(F,F[2])
induced by
the unit natural transformation from the identity functor to the composition of the two adjoint functors of tensorization with
F
and
F^{\vee}
. The composition of
u
with the trace natural transformation
tr:{\rm Ext}^{2}(F,F)\rightarrow H^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{Y})

is multiplication of
H^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{Y})
by the rank
r
of
F
(see
[
HL
, Lemma 10.1.3]
).
The obstruction map (
3.1
) restricts to an injective homomorphism from

H^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{Y})
to
{\rm Ext}^{2}(F,F)
, since
r>0
, by assumption. Consequently,
H^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{Y})
is not contained in the kernel
\Sigma(F)
of (
3.1
). The statement now follows from Lemma
3.1
.
∎

Theorem 3.4
.

Let
\Phi:D^{b}(X)\rightarrow D^{b}(Y)
be an equivalence of the derived categories of two projective irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifolds
X
and
Y
.
Let
F
be a reflexive sheaf on
Y
of rank
r>0
, which is
H
-slope-stable with respect to some ample line-bundle
H
.
Assume that
F
is isomorphic to
\Phi(G)
, where
\Sigma(G)
has co-dimension one
5
5

5

G
could be, for example,

{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
, or the sky-scraper sheaf
{\mathcal{O}}_{p}
for some point
p
in
X
, or
\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}
for a lagrangian embedding
\iota:Z\rightarrow X
as in Example
3.8
.

in
HT^{2}(X)
.
Then for every irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold
Z
deformation equivalent to
Y
there exists a possibly twisted coherent sheaf
E
over
Z
and a flat
deformation of the pair
(Y,F)
to the pair (Z,E).

Proof.

The class
\kappa_{2}(F)
remains of Hodge type
(2,2)
under every Kähler deformation of
Y
, by Proposition
3.3
. It follows that
F
is
modular
6
6

6

Let
SH^{*}(Y)
be the subalgebra of
H^{*}(Y,{\mathbb{Q}})
generated by
H^{2}(Y,{\mathbb{Q}})
. We have the decomposition of
H^{*}(Y,{\mathbb{Q}})
as an orthogonal direct sum
SH^{*}(Y)\oplus SH^{*}(Y)^{\perp}
with respect to the Poincaré paring. A torsion sheaf
F
is
modular
if the orthogonal projection of
\kappa_{2}(F)
to
SH^{*}(Y)
is a scalar multiple of the class in the image of
{\rm Sym}^{2}H^{2}(Y,{\mathbb{Q}})
corresponding to the inverse of the Beauville-Bogomolov-Fujiki pairing
[
O2
, Def. 1.1]
. The latter class is the unique class in the image of
{\rm Sym}^{2}H^{2}(Y,{\mathbb{Q}})
in
H^{4}(Y,{\mathbb{Q}})
, which remains of Hodge-type
(2,2)
under all Kähler deformations of
Y
. Hence, if
{\rm obs}_{F}
has rank
1
, then
F
is modular.

in the sense of
[
O2
, Def. 1.1]
. Hence, there exists an open subcone of the ample cone, containing
H
, such that
F
is
H^{\prime}
-slope-stable with respect to every class
H^{\prime}
in this subcone, by
[
O2
, Perop. 3.4]
.

A
\Lambda
-marking
for an irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold
Y
is an isometry
\eta:H^{2}(Y,{\mathbb{Z}})\rightarrow\Lambda
with a lattice
\Lambda
. There exists a moduli space
{\mathfrak{M}}_{\Lambda}
of
\Lambda
-marked irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifolds, which is a non-Hausdorff manifold of dimension
b_{2}(Y)-2
(see
[
Hu1
]
).
Choose an isometry
\eta:H^{2}(Y,{\mathbb{Z}})\rightarrow\Lambda
with a fixed lattice
\Lambda
. Denote by
{\mathfrak{M}}^{0}_{\Lambda}
the connected component of the moduli space
{\mathfrak{M}}_{\Lambda}
containing
(Y,\eta)
. By assumption, there exists an isometry
\eta^{\prime}:H^{2}(Z,{\mathbb{Z}})\rightarrow\Lambda
, such that
(Z,\eta^{\prime})
belongs to
{\mathfrak{M}}^{0}_{\Lambda}
.
Associated to every Kähler class
\omega
on
Y
is a twistor line
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}_{\omega}
in
{\mathfrak{M}}^{0}_{\Lambda}
through

(Y,\eta)
and a twistor family
{\mathcal{Y}}_{\omega}\rightarrow{\mathbb{P}}^{1}_{\omega}
, such that its fiber
{\mathcal{Y}}_{t}
over
t\in{\mathbb{P}}^{1}_{\omega}

represents the isomorphism class
t
as a point of
{\mathfrak{M}}^{0}_{\Lambda}

[
Hu1
]
.
A twistor path from
(Y,\eta)
to
(Z,\eta^{\prime})
is a connected sequence of twistor lines in
{\mathfrak{M}}^{0}_{\Lambda}
, such that every two consecutive lines intersect. A choice of an intersection point of every two consecutive lines is included in the data of the twistor path. If the chosen intersection points of every two consecutive lines are pairs
(Y_{i},\eta_{i})
such that
{\rm Pic}(Y_{i})
is trivial, then the twistor path is said to be generic.
There exists a generic twistor path
\gamma
from
(Y,\eta)
to
(Z,\eta^{\prime})
in
{\mathfrak{M}}_{\Lambda}^{0}
, such that its first twistor line corresponds to a Kähler class
\omega
on
Y
with respect to which
F
is slope-stable, by

[
Ve3
, Theorems 3.2 and 5.2e]
and the fact that
F
is slope-stable with respect to all ample classes in an open subcone of the ample cone.
Let
\pi:{\mathcal{Y}}\rightarrow\gamma
be the twistor deformation of
Y
over the twistor path
\gamma
.
The
first Chern class of every direct summand of
{\mathcal{E}}nd(F)
vanishes, since
F
is
H
-stable, and so
{\mathcal{E}}nd(F)
is
H
-semistable, with respect to every
H
in an open subcone of the ample cone of
Y
, see
[
Ma4
, Lemma 7.2]
.
The class
\kappa_{2}(F)
remains of Hodge type along
\gamma
, by Proposition
3.3
.
Hence, the sheaf
F
extends to a reflexive twisted sheaf
{\mathcal{F}}
over the twistor deformation
{\mathcal{Y}}
over the twistor line
\gamma
, by the generalization
[
Ma4
, Prop. 6.17]
to the case of twisted reflexive sheaves of results of Verbitsky
[
Ve5
]
for reflexive sheaves. We let
E
be the restriction of
{\mathcal{F}}
to the fiber of
\pi
over
the point
(Z,\eta^{\prime})
of the twistor path
\gamma
.
∎

Remark 3.5
.

(1)

The analogues of Lemma
3.1
and Proposition
3.3
hold when
X
is a complex torus
if we replace the assumption that
\Sigma(G)
has codimension
1
by the assumption that
\Sigma(G)
has codimension equal to

h^{0,2}(X):=\dim(H^{2,0}(X))
.
The analogue of Theorem
3.4
holds as well
if
X
is even dimensional (and so admits a hyperkähler structure).
The proof of Lemma
3.1
is identical using
Remark
2.4
instead of Lemma
2.3
. The proof of Proposition
3.3
shows that the restriction of
\exp a_{F}
to
H^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{Y})
is injective, and so
H^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{Y})
intersect
\Sigma(F)
trivially. Hence, the intersection

\Sigma(F)\cap[H^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{Y})\oplus H^{1}(TY)]
again projects onto
H^{1}(TY)
and
the hypothesis of Lemma
3.1
is satisfied.
The rest of the proof of Proposition
3.3
is identical.
The proof of Theorem
3.4
is analogous, using the same result of Verbitsky and the twistor path connectivity of the moduli space of even dimensional complex tori
[
BI
]
.

(2)

Following are examples of sheaves
G
over abelian varieties
X
for which the codimension of
\Sigma(G)
is
h^{0,2}(X)
.
Over an abelian surface every simple sheaf is an example.
Examples of such sheaves over compact complex tori of dimension
>2
include the sky scraper sheaf, a line bundle on
X
, the structure sheaf of a connected abelian subvariety, as well as their images under Fourier-Mukai transformations. When locally free sheaves, these images are semi-homogeneous vector bundles. Recall that a vector bundle
F
on
X
is
semi-homogeneous
, if every translate of
F
by a point of
X
is isomorphic to
F\otimes L
, for some line bundle
L\in{\rm Pic}^{0}(X).

If
F
is a simple semi-homogeneous vector bundle over an abelian variety
X
, then it is stable and
\dim{\rm Ext}^{2}(F,F)=h^{0,2}(X),
by
[
Mu3
, Theorem 5.8]
.
In particular, one gets via the analogue of Proposition
3.3
(with
\Phi
the identity endo-functor) an alternative proof of the fact that the graded summands
\kappa_{p}(F)\in H^{p,p}(X)
of the class
\kappa(F)
of a simple semi-homogeneous vector bundle
F
vanishes for
0<p<\dim(X)
(this also follows from
[
Mu3
, Prop. 7.3]
, which implies the vanishing of
\kappa_{\dim(X)}(F)
as well).

Lemma 3.6
.

Let
X
be an abelian variety of dimension
\geq 3
,
H
an ample line bundle on
X
, and
F
an
H
-slope-stable vector bundle. Then
F
is semi-homogeneous if and only if
the rank of the obstruction map
HT^{2}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{eq-obstruction-map})}}{{\rightarrow}}Ext^{2}(F,F)

is
h^{0,2}(X)
.

Proof.

If
F
is semi-homogeneous, then
\dim{\rm Ext}^{2}(F,F)=h^{0,2}(X),
by
by
[
Mu3
, Theorem 5.8]
.
The composition

H^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{X})\rightarrow HT^{2}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{eq-obstruction-map})}}{{\rightarrow}}Ext^{2}(F,F)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle tr}}{{\rightarrow}}H^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{X})

is multiplication by the rank of
F
, so an isomorphism, so the map (
3.1
) is surjective.
Conversely, if the co-dimension of
\Sigma(F)
in
HH^{2}(X)
is
h^{0,2}(X)
, then
\kappa_{2}(F)=0
, by Remark
3.5
(
2
), and so the statement follows from
[
O2
, Prop. A.2]
.
∎

3.2.
Examples of sheaves
F
with a rank
1
obstruction map
HT^{2}(X)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{2}(F)

Example 3.7
.

Let
E
be a simple sheaf of positive rank
r
over a
K3
surface
X
, so that
{\rm Hom}(E,E)
is one dimensional and by Serre’s Duality theorem so is
{\rm Ext}^{2}(E,E)
. Then
\Sigma(E)\subset HT^{2}(X)
satisfies the condition in Part
3.2

of Proposition
3.3
. The conclusion of the Proposition, that
\kappa(E)
remains of Hodge type, is a tautology in the
2
-dimensional case, since
\kappa(E)=r+\kappa_{2}(E)
,
\kappa_{2}(E)\in H^{4}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
, and every class in the top cohomology
H^{4}(X,{\mathbb{Q}})
remains of Hodge type under every Kähler deformation.

Example 3.8
.

Let
X
be a
2n
-dimensional irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold. Let

Z
be an
n
-dimensional compact complex manifold with Hodge numbers
h^{2,0}(Z)=0
and
h^{1,1}(Z)=1
.
Assume given a holomorphic embedding
\iota:Z\hookrightarrow X
.
Then
\iota(Z)
is a lagrangian submanifold.
The obstruction map
HT^{2}(X)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{2}(\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z},\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z})

has rank
1
, by Lemma
3.9
below.
Every smooth curve
Z
in a
K3
surface is an example.
Examples where
Z={\mathbb{P}}^{n}
are studied in
[
Ba
,
HT
]
.
Examples with
Z
a Grassmannian
G(k,2k+1)
are given in
[
Ma1
, Example 3.3]
.
Every smooth cubic fourfold
V
that does not contain a plane
embeds as a lagrangian subvariety of the projective irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold of
K3^{[4]}
deformation type parametrizing equivalence classes of generalized twisted cubics in
V

[
LLSv
, Theorem B]
.

Lemma 3.9
.

Let
\iota:Z\rightarrow X
be as in Example
3.8
.
Then
{\rm Ext}^{2}(\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z},\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z})
is one-dimensional and the obstruction map
HT^{2}(X)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{2}(\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z},\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z})

has rank
1
.

Proof.

The normal bundle
N_{Z/X}
is isomorphic to
\Omega^{1}_{Z}
, as
Z
is a lagrangian subvariety, and so
the extension sheaf
{\mathcal{E}}xt^{i}(\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z},\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z})

is isomorphic to
\Omega^{i}_{Z}
.
We have the seven-terms exact sequence of the local to global spectral sequence, where
N=N_{Z/X}
:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle H^{1}(\wedge^{0}N)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{1}_{X}(\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z},\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z})\rightarrow H^{0}(N)

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle H^{2}(\wedge^{0}N)\rightarrow\ker\left[{\rm Ext}^{2}_{X}(\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z},\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z})\rightarrow H^{0}(\wedge^{2}N)\right]\rightarrow H^{1}(N)\rightarrow H^{3}(\wedge^{0}N)

[
Mi
, Appendix B, page 309]
.
Our assumptions imply that
H^{0}(\wedge^{2}N)
and
H^{2}(\wedge^{0}N)
vanish as they are isomorphic to

H^{0}(\Omega^{2}_{Z})
and
H^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{Z})
.
It follows that
{\rm Ext}^{2}(\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z},\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z})\rightarrow H^{1}(Z,N_{Z/X})\cong H^{1}(Z,\Omega^{1}_{Z})
is injective and so
{\rm Ext}^{2}(\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z},\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z})
is at most one-dimensional.

We claim that
{\rm Ext}^{2}(\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z},\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z})
does not vanish and the obstruction map (
3.1
) restricts to a non-trivial, hence surjective, homomorphism from
H^{1}(TX)
onto
{\rm Ext}^{2}(\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z},\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z})
.
Assume, otherwise, that
H^{1}(TX)
is contained in the kernel of the obstruction map.
Then
ch(\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z})
remains of Hodge type in the direction of every
\xi\in H^{1}(TX)
, by
Remark
3.2
. Hence, so does the cohomology class
[Z]\in H^{n,n}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})
of
\iota(Z)
,
since
[Z]
is a graded direct summand of
ch(\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z})
.
Let
T_{Z}X
be the subsheaf of
TX
of vector fields which are tangent to
\iota(Z)
. So
T_{Z}X
is the kernel in the short exact sequence

0\rightarrow T_{Z}X\rightarrow TX\rightarrow\iota_{*}(N_{Z/X})\rightarrow 0.

H^{1}(X,T_{Z}X)
is the space of first order complex deformations of the pair
(X,\iota(Z))
.
It is known that the pair
(X,\iota(Z))
deforms with
X
over a co-dimension
1
smooth locus in the local Kuranishi deformation space of
X
and the sequence

H^{1}(X,T_{Z}X)\rightarrow H^{1}(X,TX)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle c_{1}(L)}}{{\rightarrow}}H^{2}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X})\rightarrow 0

is exact, where
L
is the line bundle on
X
such that the kernel of
H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{C}})\rightarrow H^{2}(Z,{\mathbb{C}})
is orthogonal to
c_{1}(L)
via the BBF-pairing
[
Vo1
, Cor. 1.2]
. In particular, the left homomorphism above has a one-dimensional co-kernel.
Furthermore, the cohomology class
[Z]
remains of Hodge type in a direction
\xi\in H^{1}(TX)
,
if and only if
c_{1}(L)
does
[
Vo1
, Cor. 1.4]
. A contradiction.
We conclude that
{\rm Ext}^{2}(\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z},\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z})
does not vanish and

\Sigma(\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z})
has co-dimension
1
in
HT^{2}(X)
.
∎

Remark 3.10
.

(1)

Let
X
be a
2n
-dimensional irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold,

Z
an
n
-dimensional compact complex manifold, and
\iota:Z\hookrightarrow X
an embedding as a lagrangian submanifold.
Let
{\mathcal{K}}
be a line bundle on
Z
satisfying
{\mathcal{K}}^{k}\cong\omega_{Z}^{j}
for some integers
j,k
with
k\neq 0
. Set
F:=\iota_{*}{\mathcal{K}}
.
We expect the obstruction map
{\rm obs}^{HT}_{F}
, given in (
3.1
), to have rank
1
whenever the restriction homomorphism

H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{C}})\rightarrow H^{2}(Z,{\mathbb{C}})
has rank
1
.
The latter condition is equivalent to the homomorphism
H^{1,1}(X)\rightarrow H^{1,1}(Z)
having rank
1
.
Equivalently, the homomorphism
H^{1}(X,TX)\rightarrow H^{1}(Z,N_{Z/X})
, induced by the
sheaf homomorphism
TX\rightarrow\iota_{*}N_{Z/X}
, has rank
1
, as the symplectic structure of
X
induces a commutative diagram

\textstyle{TX\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\iota_{*}N_{Z/X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\Omega^{1}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\iota_{*}\Omega^{1}_{Z}.}

The domain
HT^{2}(X)
of
{\rm obs}^{HT}_{F}
admits a direct sum decomposition by definition and its co-domain
{\rm Ext}^{2}(F,F)
decomposes as a direct sum

\bigoplus_{p+q=2}H^{p}(X,\wedge^{q}N_{Z/X})
, by the degeneration of the local-to-global spectral sequence at the
{}^{2}E
-page

[
Ml
, Theorem 0.1.3]
.
The
homomorphism

H^{2}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X})\rightarrow H^{2}(X,{\mathcal{E}}xt^{0}_{X}(\iota_{*}{\mathcal{K}},\iota_{*}{\mathcal{K}}))\cong H^{2}(Z,{\mathcal{O}}_{Z})
vanishes, due to
Z
being lagrangian.
The sheaf homomorphism
TX\rightarrow\iota_{*}N_{Z/X}
induces the zero homomorphism

H^{0}(X,\wedge^{2}TX)\rightarrow H^{0}(Z,\wedge^{2}N_{Z/X})\cong H^{0}(Z,{\mathcal{E}}xt^{2}_{X}(\iota_{*}{\mathcal{K}},\iota_{*}{\mathcal{K}})),

again due to
Z
being lagrangian. However,
the obstruction map (
3.1
) need not factor through
the direct sum of the
above homomorphisms.

(2)

In order to prove that the obstruction map has rank
1
it suffices to prove that the image of
H^{0}(X,\wedge^{2}TX)
via the
obstruction map is contained in the image of
H^{1}(X,TX)
.
When
{\mathcal{K}}^{2}\cong\omega_{Z}
the deformation quantization result of
[
DS
]
seems to suggest that
H^{0}(X,\wedge^{2}TX)
is contained in the kernel of the obstruction map and so the latter has rank
1
. It follows that whenever a square root of
\omega_{Z}
belongs to the image of
\iota^{*}{\rm Pic}(X)\rightarrow{\rm Pic}(Z)
, then the obstruction map

{\rm obs}^{HT}_{F}
has rank
1
for
F=\iota_{*}\iota^{*}L
, for every
L\in{\rm Pic}(X)
, since if
\iota^{*}(L_{0})^{2}\cong\omega_{Z}
, then
tensorization by
L\otimes L_{0}^{-1}
is an auto-equivalnce of
D^{b}(X)
mapping
\iota_{*}\iota^{*}L_{0}
to
\iota_{*}\iota^{*}L
and so the rank of the obstruction map for
F=\iota_{*}\iota^{*}L
is equal to that for
F=\iota_{*}\iota^{*}L_{0}
.

(3)

If
Z
is a lagrangian connected component of the fixed locus of a finite group
G
acting on
X
, then the image of
H^{0}(X,\wedge^{2}TX)
via the obstruction map
{\rm obs}^{HT}_{\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}}
vanishes and the obstruction map has rank
1
. This is seen as follows.
The
universal Atiyah class

a_{X}
is the extension class of the short exact sequence

(3.3)

0\rightarrow I_{\Delta_{X}}/I^{2}_{\Delta_{X}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{X\times X}/I^{2}_{\Delta_{X}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{X\times X}/I_{\Delta_{X}}\rightarrow 0

over
X\times X
. It induces a natural transformation from the identity endofunctor of
D^{b}(X)
to the endofunctor of tensorization by
\Omega^{1}_{X}[1]
. We denote by
a_{F}\in{\rm Hom}(F,F\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X}[1])
the value of
a_{X}
at an object
F
of
D^{b}(X)
.
The Atiyah class
a_{\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}}\in{\rm Ext}^{1}_{X}(\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z},\iota_{*}\iota^{*}(\Omega^{1}_{X}))
is thus
the extension class of the short exact sequence of
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-modules

(3.4)

0\rightarrow\iota_{*}\iota^{*}(\Omega^{1}_{X})\rightarrow E\rightarrow\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}\rightarrow 0,

involving the Fourier-Mukai transforms of
\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}
with respect to the objects in
(
3.3
). Note that the scheme theoretic support of
E
is the (non-reducded) first order neighborhood of
Z
in
X
.
The exact sequence (
3.4
)
decomposes as a direct sum of (a) the short exact sequence of the
G
-invariant direct summands,

0\rightarrow\iota_{*}\Omega^{1}_{Z}\rightarrow E^{G}\rightarrow\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}\rightarrow 0,

and (b) the short exact sequence

0\rightarrow N^{*}_{Z/X}\rightarrow N^{*}_{Z/X}\rightarrow 0\rightarrow 0.

Hence,
a_{\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}}:\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}\rightarrow\iota_{*}\iota^{*}\Omega^{1}_{X}[1]

factors through a morphism
7
7

7

This morphism corresponds to the extension class as
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-modules and is different in general from the extension class as
{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}
-modules.

\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}\rightarrow\iota_{*}\Omega^{1}_{Z}[1]

via the inclusion of
\iota_{*}\Omega^{1}_{Z}[1]
as the
G
-invariant direct summand of
\iota_{*}\iota^{*}\Omega^{1}_{X}[1]
.
It follows that
a_{\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z},2}:\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}\rightarrow\iota_{*}\iota^{*}\Omega^{2}_{X}[2]
factors through
the direct summand
\iota_{*}\Omega^{2}_{Z}[2]
and so the image of
H^{0}(X,\wedge^{2}TX)
via the obstruction map (
3.1
) vanishes, since the homomorphism

\iota^{*}\Omega^{2}_{X}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}
induced by an element of the non-trivial
1
-dimensional
G
-representation
H^{0}(X,\wedge^{2}TX)
vanishes on the
G
-invariant direct summand
\Omega^{2}_{Z}
.

(4)

We will verify in Lemma
7.3
that the rank of the cohomological obstruction map is
1
(a condition slightly weaker than the rank of the obstruction map being
1
) in some examples of lagrangian surfaces.

Example 3.11
.

Following are examples of embeddings
\iota:Z\rightarrow X
of holomorphic lagrangian submanifolds
Z
with a rank
1
restriction homomorphism
\iota^{*}:H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})\rightarrow H^{2}(Z,{\mathbb{Z}})
even though the second Betti number of
Z
is larger than
1
.

(1)

Z
is a lagrangian complex torus. In this case the kernel of
H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})\rightarrow H^{2}(Z,{\mathbb{Z}})
is
c_{1}(L)^{\perp}
, for a line bundle
L
with isotropic
c_{1}(L)

[
W
]
. For a generic pair
(X,Z)
some power
d
of the line bundle
L
is base point free and
the morphism
\varphi_{L^{d}}:X\rightarrow{\mid}L^{d}{\mid}^{*}
is a lagrangian fibration onto its image with fiber
Z

[
GLR
]
. Hence,
[Z]
is a scalar multiple of
c_{1}(L)^{n}
, where
n=\dim(X)/2
. If
X
is of
K3^{[n]}
-type, then
d=1
and
\varphi_{L}
is surjective, so
[Z]=c_{1}(L)^{n}
, by
[
Ma5
]
.

(2)

Let
W
be a
6
dimensional complex vector space and
let
V\subset{\mathbb{P}}(W)
be a smooth cubic fourfold. Let
X
be the Fano variety of lines on
V
. Then
X
is of
K3^{[2]}
-type, by
[
BD
]
. Let
Z
be the Fano variety of lines on a smooth hyperplane section of
V
. Then
Z
is a lagrangian surface in
X
, by
[
Vo1
]
.
The Hodge numbers of
Z
are
h^{1,0}=5
,
h^{2,0}=10
, and
h^{1,1}=25
, and
\chi(Z)=27
, by

[
CG
, Eq. (9.5), (9.12), and (10.12)]
.
Note that
Z
is the zero subscheme of a regular section of
\tau^{*}
, where
\tau
is the restriction to
X
of the tautological rank
2
subbundle over
Gr(2,6)
. Hence,
[Z]=c_{2}(\tau^{*})
.
Let
L
be the restriction to
X
of the ample generator of
{\rm Pic}(Gr(2,6))
. The restriction of
L
to
Z
is the canonical line bundle of
Z
, by
[
CG
, Prop. 10.3]
.
We have
(c_{1}(L),c_{1}(L))=6
.
One can show that

[Z]=\frac{1}{8}(5c_{1}(L)^{2}-c_{2}(TX))
.
If
\tilde{\tau}:W\rightarrow W
is an involution with a
5
-dimensional
1
-eigenspace
W^{+}
and
V
is
\tilde{\tau}
-invariant
and we choose
Z
to be the Fano variety of lines on
V\cap{\mathbb{P}}(W^{+})
, then
Z
is a connected component
8
8

8

The fixed locus consists of two connected components, the other being a cubic surface, see
[
Be2
, Sec. 3.6]
.

of the fixed locus of the restriction
\tau:X\rightarrow X
of
\tilde{\tau}
and so the obstruction map
HT^{2}(X)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{2}(\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z},\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z})
given in (
3.1
) has rank
1
, by Remark
3.10
(
3
).

An example of an immersion of a smooth surface as a lagrangian subvariety in
X
of class
63c_{2}(\tau^{*})
with a finite number of self intersection points is studied in
[
IM1
]
.

(3)

Let
X
be of
K3^{[2]}
-type admitting an ample line-bundle
L
satisfying
(c_{1}(L),c_{1}(L))=2
. The generic such
X
admits an anti-symplectic involution. O’Grady proved that its fixed locus, which is a lagrangian surface
Z
, embeds in
{\mid}L{\mid}^{*}
as a smooth surface of degree
40
and Euler characteristic
192

[
O1
]
. One can show that

[Z]=5c_{1}(L)^{2}-\frac{1}{3}c_{2}(TX)
. The obstruction map
HT^{2}(X)\rightarrow{\rm Ext}^{2}(\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z},\iota_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{Z})
given in (

) has rank

, by Remark
3.10
(
3
).

Example 3.12
.

Let
X
be a
K3
surface containing a smooth rational curve
C
, let
\delta:X\rightarrow X\times X
be the diagonal embedding,
and let
\pi_{i}
,
i=1,2
, be the projection from
X\times X
onto the
i
-th factor.
The shift
{\mathcal{I}}_{\Delta}[1]
of the ideal sheaf of the diagonal
\Delta:=\delta(X)
is quasi-isomorphic to the complex

{\mathcal{P}}:=[{\mathcal{O}}_{X\times X}\rightarrow\delta_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}]
, with
\delta_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
in degree
0
, and the latter is the Fourier-Mukai kernel of the auto-equivalence
\Phi^{\mathcal{P}}:=R\pi_{1,*}\circ({\mathcal{P}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{\otimes}}L\pi_{1}^{*}(\bullet))
of
D^{b}(X)
known as the spherical twist by the spherical object
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}

[
ST
]
.

\Phi^{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{O}}_{X}(C))
is isomorphic to the co-kernel of the evaluation homomorphism

H^{0}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(C))\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(C)
and is thus the push-forward
\iota_{*}N_{C/X}
of the normal bundle of
C
by the inclusion
\iota:C\rightarrow X
.
Thus, the composition of
\Phi^{\mathcal{P}}
with tensorization by
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(C)
is
an auto-equivalence
\Psi
of the derived category of
X
which maps
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
to
\iota_{*}N_{C/X}
. Note the isomorphism
\iota_{*}N_{C/X}\cong\iota_{*}\omega_{C}
.
Clearly, the latter sheaf deforms with
X
only over the locus in the moduli space where the class of
C
in
H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{C}})
remains of Hodge type. In this case we have

\Sigma(\Psi({\mathcal{O}}_{X}))\cap\left[H^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{X})\oplus H^{1}(TX)\right]=\Sigma(\iota_{*}\omega_{C})\cap\left[H^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{X})\oplus H^{1}(TX)\right]=H^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{X})\oplus[C]^{\perp},

where
[C]^{\perp}
is the hyperplane of
H^{1}(TX)
along which
C
remains of type
(1,1)
. The second equality above si due to the fact that the right hand side is the unique hyperplane of
H^{2}({\mathcal{O}}_{X})\oplus H^{1}(TX)
projecting onto
[C]^{\perp}
.

3.3.
When can a very modular sheaf on
X
be deformed to an untwisted sheaf on
Y
?

Let
X
and
Y
be deformation equivalent irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifolds.
Let
E
be be a reflexive sheaf of positive rank
r
on
X
with a rank
1
obstruction map
{\rm obs}^{HT}_{E}
, given in
(
3.1
). Assume that
E
is slope-stable with respect to Kähler classes in an open subcone of the Kähler cone of
X
.
Then
E
can be deformed to a twisted sheaf
F
over
Y
, by Theorem
3.4
. When can the Azumaya algebra
{\mathcal{E}}nd(E)
be deformed to the Azumaya algebra
{\mathcal{E}}nd(F)
of
an untwisted sheaf
F
over
Y
? The answer depends on
c_{1}(E)
and

{\rm Pic}(Y)
.
Lemma
3.13
provides a general necessary and sufficient condition and Lemma
3.16
provides a condition in a form easier to check for
Y
of
K3^{[n]}
or generalized Kummer deformation type.

Lemma 3.13
.

There exists an untwisted reflexive sheaf
F
such that
(Y,{\mathcal{E}}nd(F))
is deformation equivalent to
(X,{\mathcal{E}}nd(E))
, if and only if there exists a parallel transport operator
g:H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})\rightarrow H^{2}(Y,{\mathbb{Z}})
, such that
g(c_{1}(E))+rH^{2}(Y,{\mathbb{Z}})

belongs to the image of
H^{1,1}(Y,{\mathbb{Z}})
in
H^{2}(Y,{\mathbb{Z}})/rH^{2}(Y,{\mathbb{Z}})
.

Proof.

The existence of such a parallel-transport operator
g
is clearly necessary. We prove that it is sufficient.
Assume first that
F
is locally free. Let
\mu_{r}
be the group of
r
-th roots of unity. Denote by
\mu_{r}
also the associated trivial local system over
X
.
Let

\tilde{\theta}:H^{1}_{an}(X,PGL_{r}({\mathcal{O}}))\rightarrow H^{2}(X,\mu_{r})

be the connecting homomorphism of the short exact sequence

0\rightarrow\mu_{r}\rightarrow SL_{r}({\mathcal{O}})\rightarrow PGL_{r}({\mathcal{O}})\rightarrow 0.

The projective bundle
{\mathbb{P}}(E)
determines a class
[{\mathbb{P}}(E)]
in
H^{1}(X,PGL_{r}({\mathcal{O}}))
.
The exponential function
\exp:{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{*}
maps the subgroup
\frac{2\pi i}{r}{\mathbb{Z}}
of
{\mathbb{C}}
onto
\mu_{r}
and induces the homomorphism

\exp:H^{2}\left(X,\frac{2\pi i}{r}{\mathbb{Z}}\right)\rightarrow H^{2}(X,\mu_{r}).

The following equality is proved in
[
HSc
, Lemma 2.5]

(3.5)

\tilde{\theta}([{\mathbb{P}}(E)])=\exp\left(\frac{2\pi i}{r}c_{1}(E)\right).

Choose an isometry
\eta:H^{2}(X,{\mathbb{Z}})\rightarrow\Lambda
with a fixed lattice
\Lambda
.
Let
{\mathfrak{M}}_{\Lambda}
be the moduli space of marked irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifolds recalled in the proof of Theorem
3.4
.
Let
{\mathfrak{M}}_{\Lambda}^{0}
be the connected component of
{\mathfrak{M}}_{\Lambda}
containing
(X,\eta)
. Then

(Y,g\circ\eta)
belongs to
{\mathfrak{M}}_{\Lambda}^{0}
, since
g
is a parallel-transport operator. Choose a generic twistor path
\gamma
from
(X,\eta)
to
(Y,g\circ\eta)
with first twistor line associated to a Kähler class with respect to which
F
is slope-stable. Then
{\mathbb{P}}(E)
deforms along
\gamma
to a projective bundle
B
with

(3.6)

\tilde{\theta}([B])=\exp\left(\frac{2\pi i}{r}g(c_{1}(E))\right).

Let
\iota:\mu_{r}\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{X}^{*}
be the inclusion.
There exists a locally free sheaf
F
over
Y
with
{\mathbb{P}}(F)=B
, if and only if the Brauer class
