of the Mukai lattice of

orthogonal to

, by
[

, Lemma 10.14]
.
The isometry

is the image
{mon}(\gamma)
via the parallel-transport representation
{mon}
in
[
Ma8
, Theorem 1.6]

of the isometry
\gamma:=-D\circ R_{(1,0,1)}
of the Mukai lattice, where

and

is the reflection with respect to the sublattice orthogonal to

[

, Theorem 3.21]
.
Now,
{mon}(\gamma)

corresponds, via the Mukai isometries

and
\theta_{w}
, to the isometry
-\gamma_{|}:v^{\perp}\rightarrow w^{\perp}
, which is minus
the restriction of
the isometry
\gamma
.
The isometry

sends

to

and sends the class
(2,h,2)
to itself. Hence, the class of

in

H^{2}(M(v),{\mathbb{Z}})
is

.

The class of the divisor of non-reduced subschemes in

is

, where
\delta:=\theta_{v}((1,h,4))
.
The nef class

orthogonal to

, corresponding to the class

, is

\tilde{h}=\theta_{v}((0,h,6))
. The equality

follows.

(
2
), (
3
)
The extremal rays associated to the two divisorial contractions correspond via the isomorphism
H^{2}(M(v),{\mathbb{Q}})\rightarrow H_{2}(M(v),{\mathbb{Q}})
,

, to the rays spanned by the classes

and

(see
[

, Cor. 3.6]
).
The nef cone of

is thus dual to the cone generated by the classes

and
\delta
.
F(X)
is a subvariety of

and

pulls back via

to a nef class

satisfying

and

.
Hence

. The equality

follows.
The nef cone is thus

.

(
4
)
The restriction of

and of

to

are equal, as

and

.
∎

We are ready to prove Proposition
13.2
.

Proof.

The surface
g(F(C))
is smooth and lagrangian, by Lemma
13.10
. Let
L
be the line bundle over
S^{[2]}
with class
\lambda
, so that
L=f^{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{F(X)}(1)
, by Lemma
13.12
. The ampleness of the restriction of
L
to
g(F(C))
and
Lemma
13.10
imply that
the object
\Phi^{[2]}_{\mathcal{P}}(L^{k}\otimes g_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{F(C)})

satisfies the three conditions of Lemma
13.8
,
with
{\mathcal{L}}=L^{k}
, for all
k
sufficiently large, and
is hence isomorphic to a torsion free reflexive sheaf.

The pair
(S^{[2]},g_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{F(C)})
is the limit of a flat family of pairs
(F(X_{t}),{\mathcal{O}}_{F(X_{t}\cap H_{t})})
, where
X_{t}
is a smooth cubic fourfold and
X_{t}\cap H_{t}
is a smooth hyperplane section, as
S^{[2]}
is the minimal resolution of an
A_{1}
singularity along the embedding of
S
as the singular locus of
F(X)
, by Claim
13.11
. Such a flat family is obtained from a flat deformation of
F(X)
after passage to a double cover of the base of that family, see
[
Ma6
]
. (This was the main point of Mukai’s
[
Mu1
, Example 4.3]
, as remarked in
[
Mu1
, Footnote 38]
).
The sheaf
g_{*}{\mathcal{O}}_{F(C)}
thus has a rank
1
cohomological obstruction map,
by Lemma
7.3
and Example
7.4
.

E_{k}
has a rank
1
cohomological obstruction map
as well, as the property is invariant under derived equivalences.

The exact sequence (
13.2
) is obtained from
(
13.16
), with
{\mathcal{L}}=L^{k}
,
by adding the kernel of the homomorphism
a_{\chi}
and using the equality of the restrictions to
Z=g(F(C))
of
\delta
and of
L

established in Lemma
13.12
.
The rank of
F_{k}
is 12, by Lemma
13.10
.
The rank of
E_{k}
, for
k
sufficiently large, is thus

-12+\dim H^{0}(Z,{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(k+1))+\dim H^{0}(Z,{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(k)).

The multiplication table (
6.19
) and
Lemma
7.1
, with
(\lambda,\lambda)=6
,
c=5/8
, and
t=1
as in Example
7.4
, yields the third equality below.

\displaystyle\chi({\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(k))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{S^{[2]}}ch({\mathcal{O}}_{Z})\exp(k\lambda)td_{X}=\chi({\mathcal{O}}_{Z})+\frac{k^{2}}{2}\int_{S^{[2]}}[Z]\lambda^{2}+k\int_{S^{[2]}}ch_{3}({\mathcal{O}}_{Z}))\lambda

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 6+\frac{45k^{2}}{2}-\frac{45k}{2}=6+45\frac{k(k-1)}{2}.

Hence, the rank of
E_{k}
, for
k
sufficiently large, is
45k^{2}
.

It remains to calculate
\ell(E_{k})
and
c_{1}(E_{k})
. The latter is determined by
{\rm rank}(E_{k})
and the former, by Theorem
6.13
(
3
).
Now

\ell({\mathcal{O}}_{Z})=(0,\lambda,-3)
,
by Lemma
7.3
,

\ell({\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(k))=B_{k\lambda}(0,\lambda,-3)=(0,\lambda,6k-3)
, and

\ell(E_{k})
is the image via
\Phi^{[2]}_{{\mathcal{P}},\chi}=\Phi^{[2]}_{\chi}\circ\Phi^{[2]}_{\mathcal{P}}
of
\ell({\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(k))
.

\Phi_{\mathcal{P}}
acts on the Mukai lattice of
S
via
\phi_{\mathcal{P}}(r,\alpha,s)=(s,-\alpha,r)
.
According to
[
Ta
, Prop. 9.3]

\Phi_{\mathcal{P}}^{[2]}
acts on the LLV lattice of
S^{[2]}
by

\widetilde{H}(\phi_{\mathcal{P}}^{[2]})=\det(\phi_{\mathcal{P}})(B_{-\delta/2}\circ\iota(\phi_{\mathcal{P}})\circ B_{\delta/2}),

where
\iota(\phi_{\mathcal{P}})
leaves the class
\delta\in H^{2}(S^{[2]})
invariant and acts of
\delta^{\perp}
by
\phi_{\mathcal{P}}
via the natural identification
of
\delta^{\perp}
with the LLV lattice of
S
. Note that
\det(\phi_{\mathcal{P}})=-1
,
\lambda=2\tilde{h}-3\delta
,
(\delta/2,\lambda)=3
,

B_{\delta/2}(0,\lambda,6k-3)=(0,\lambda,6k)
, and

\iota(\phi_{\mathcal{P}})(0,\lambda,6k)=(6k,-2\tilde{h}-3\delta,0)
. Hence,

\widetilde{H}(\phi_{\mathcal{P}}^{[2]})(0,\lambda,6k-3)=-B_{-\delta/2}(6k,-2\tilde{h}-3\delta,0)=-(6k,-2\tilde{h}-(3+3k)\delta,-3-(3/2)k).

Let
\phi^{[2]}_{\chi}:H^{*}(S^{[2]},{\mathbb{Q}})\rightarrow H^{*}(S^{[2]},{\mathbb{Q}})
be the automorphism induced by
\Phi^{[2]}_{\chi}
.
Let
\widetilde{H}(\phi^{[2]}_{\chi}):\widetilde{H}(S^{[2]},{\mathbb{Q}})\rightarrow\widetilde{H}(S^{[2]},{\mathbb{Q}})
be the induced isometry via
(
5.2
), which appeared in Lemma
12.1
.
Lemma
12.1
yields
the equality
\widetilde{H}(\phi_{\chi}^{[2]}\circ\phi_{\mathcal{P}}^{[2]})=-R_{(0,\delta,1)}\circ\widetilde{H}(\phi_{\mathcal{P}}^{[2]})
, and so

\widetilde{H}(\phi_{\chi}^{[2]}\circ\phi_{\mathcal{P}}^{[2]})(0,\lambda,6k-1)))=\left(6k,-2\tilde{h}-(3k-3)\delta,3-\frac{3}{2}k\right).

Thus,
c_{1}(E_{k})=-15k\tilde{h}-\frac{45k(k-1)}{2}\delta
, by Theorem
6.13
(
3
).
∎

Remark 13.13
.

Let
a^{\prime}_{k}
be the restriction of
a_{k}
to the first direct summand in (
13.2
) and let
a_{k}^{\prime\prime}
be its restriction to the second direct summand.
Then
G^{\prime}_{k}:=\ker(a^{\prime}_{k})
and
G^{\prime\prime}_{k}:=\ker(a^{\prime\prime}_{k})
are saturated subsheaves of
E_{k}
and
G^{\prime\prime}_{k}\cong G^{\prime}_{k-1}(-\delta)
. Let us determine the open subcone of the ample cone of
S^{[2]}
, where
G^{\prime}_{k}
and
G^{\prime\prime}_{k}
do not slope-destabilize
E_{k}
, when

{\rm Pic}(S)
is cyclic. In that case the nef cone of
S^{[2]}
is
\langle\tilde{h},2\tilde{h}-3\delta\rangle
, by Lemma
13.12
. Hence, the class
\kappa:=j\tilde{h}-\delta
,
j\in{\mathbb{Q}}
, is ample precisely when
j>2/3
and every rational ample class is a multiple of such a
\kappa
.
The
normalized
\kappa
-slope
of a sheaf
F
on
S^{[2]}
of positive rank
r
is

\mu_{\kappa}(F):=(\kappa,c_{1}(F))/r
. The equality

\int_{S^{[2]}}\kappa^{3}c_{1}(F)=3(\kappa,\kappa)(\kappa,c_{1}(F))

implies that the slope of
F
is
3(\kappa,\kappa)\mu_{\kappa}(F)
, and is hence proportional to
\mu_{\kappa}(F)
by a positive constant independent of
F
.
The normalized
\kappa
-slopes with respect to
\kappa
of the three sheaves are as follows.

\begin{array}[]{cccc}\underline{\mbox{sheaf}}&\underline{\mbox{rank}}&\underline{\hskip 43.05542ptc_{1}\hskip 43.05542pt}&\underline{\hskip 34.44434pt\mu_{\kappa}\hskip 34.44434pt}\\
E_{k}&45k^{2}&-15k\tilde{h}-\frac{45k(k-1)}{2}\delta&-\left(\frac{2j+k-1}{k}\right)\\
G^{\prime}_{k}&-6+\frac{45k(k+1)}{2}&-15k\tilde{h}+6\delta&\frac{-60kj+8}{15k(k+1)-4}\\
G^{\prime\prime}_{k}&-6+\frac{45k(k-1)}{2}&-15(k-1)\tilde{h}+\left(12-\frac{45k(k-1)}{2}\right)\delta&\frac{16-(k-1)[30k+60j]}{15k(k-1)-4}\end{array}

The inequality
\mu_{\kappa}(G^{\prime}_{k})>\mu_{\kappa}(G^{\prime\prime}_{k})
holds, for
k\geq 2
.
Hence, for

j>\frac{k+1}{2}

and
k\geq 2
the normalized
\kappa
-slopes
\mu_{\kappa}(G^{\prime}_{k})
and
\mu_{\kappa}(G^{\prime\prime}_{k})
are smaller than
\mu_{\kappa}(E_{k})
.

Lemma 13.14
.

We have the short exact sequence

(13.19)

0\rightarrow G^{\prime}_{k}\rightarrow E_{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eta^{\prime}}}{{\rightarrow}}H^{0}(Z,{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(k))\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}{\mathcal{O}}_{S^{[2]}}(-\delta)\rightarrow 0,

where the homomorphism
\eta^{\prime}

is the natural one arising from the isomorphism

H^{0}(Z,{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(k))\cong{\rm Hom}(E_{k},{\mathcal{O}}_{S^{[2]}}(-\delta))^{*}
.
The sheaf
G^{\prime}_{k}
is isomorphic to the image via
\Phi^{[2]}_{{\mathcal{P}}.\chi}
of the object

(13.20)

{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(k)\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{S^{[2]}}[4]\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}{\rm Hom}\left({\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(k),{\mathcal{O}}_{S^{[2]}}[4]\right)^{*}

in degrees
0
and
1
.

Proof.

The exact sequence (
13.19
)
follows from the Snake Lemma and the commutative diagram with short exact rows the second of which is (
13.2
)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G^{\prime}_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{0}(Z,{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(k+1))\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}{\mathcal{O}}_{S^{[2]}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{k}^{\prime}}
\textstyle{F_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{id}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\left[H^{0}(Z,{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(k+1))\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}{\mathcal{O}}_{S^{[2]}}\right]\oplus\left[H^{0}(Z,{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(k))\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}{\mathcal{O}}_{S^{[2]}}(-\delta)\right]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{k}}
\textstyle{F_{k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

The exactness of both rows follows from Proposition
13.2
.

E_{k}
is isomorphic to

\Phi^{[2]}_{{\mathcal{P}}.\chi}({\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(k))
and
{\mathcal{O}}_{S^{[2]}}(-\delta)
is isomorphic to

\Phi^{[2]}_{{\mathcal{P}}.\chi}({\mathcal{O}}_{S^{[2]}}[4])
,
by Lemma
13.5
.
The morphism
\eta^{\prime}
is the image via
\Phi^{[2]}_{{\mathcal{P}}.\chi}
of the natural morphism in
(
13.20
),
where we identify
H^{0}(Z,{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(k))
and
{\rm Hom}\left({\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(k),{\mathcal{O}}_{S^{[2]}}[4]\right)^{*}

via Serre’s duality and the triviality of the canonical line bundle of
S^{[2]}
.
∎

Lemma 13.15
.

We have the exact sequence

0\rightarrow G^{\prime\prime}_{k}\rightarrow E_{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eta^{\prime\prime}}}{{\rightarrow}}H^{0}(Z,{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(k+1))\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}{\mathcal{O}}_{S^{[2]}}\rightarrow F_{k}/F_{k-1}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{S^{[2]}}(-\delta)\rightarrow 0.

The homomorphism
\eta^{\prime\prime}
is the natural one under the identification of
H^{0}(Z,{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(k+1))

with
{\rm Hom}(E_{k},{\mathcal{O}}_{S^{[2]}})^{*}
.

Proof.

The exact sequence is obtained via the Snake Lemma after replacing the first row in the diagram in Lemma
13.14
by

0\rightarrow G^{\prime\prime}_{k}\rightarrow H^{0}(Z,{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(k))\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}{\mathcal{O}}_{S^{[2]}}(-\delta)\rightarrow F_{k-1}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{S^{[2]}}(-\delta)\rightarrow 0.

The trivial line bundle is isomorphic to

\Phi^{[2]}_{{\mathcal{P}}.\chi}({\mathcal{O}}_{S^{[2]}}(-\delta)[4])
,
by Lemma
13.5
.
The morphism
\eta^{\prime\prime}
is the image via
\Phi^{[2]}_{{\mathcal{P}}.\chi}
of the natural homomorphism

{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(k)\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}_{S^{[2]}}(-\delta)[4]\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}{\rm Hom}({\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(k),{\mathcal{O}}_{S^{[2]}}(-\delta)[4])^{*},

where we identify
H^{0}(Z,{\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(k+1))
with
{\rm Hom}({\mathcal{O}}_{Z}(k),{\mathcal{O}}_{S^{[2]}}(-\delta)[4])^{*}

via Serre’s duality, the triviality of the canonical line bundle of
S^{[2]}
and Lemma
13.12
(
4
).
∎
